# Preface

The present progress report covers research and development activities using the HI-13 tandem accelerator at the Beijing National Tandem Accelerator Laboratory (BNTAL) for calendar year of 2005. During this period, the HI-13 tandem accelerator was operated smoothly and delivered ion beams over 4 000 hours, on the target, to the experiments in the fields of nuclear data measurement $(20\%)$ , nuclear reaction (including nuclear astrophysics, $24\%$ ), nuclear structure $(22\%)$ , and nuclear application $(34\%)$ . Some main research progress was summarized as follows.

# 1 Nuclear Structure

The level schemes of $^{178}\mathrm{Os}$ , $^{152}\mathrm{Gd}$ , $^{137}\mathrm{La}$ , $^{136}\mathrm{Ce}$ , $^{106}\mathrm{Ag}$ and $^{52}\mathrm{Mn}$ were investigated and the half life of the levels was deduced. Signature inversion in doubly-odd nucleus $^{158}\mathrm{Tm}$ was observed. Measurement of $g$ -factors of magnetic dipole states in $^{85}\mathrm{Zr}$ and measurement of $^{7}\mathrm{Be}$ decay rate in Au were finished.

# 2 Nuclear Reactions

The excitation functions of quasielastic scattering at backward angles were measured for the system of $^{32}\mathrm{S} + ^{90,96}\mathrm{Zr}$ . The barrier distributions were extracted from these measured excitation functions. The measured barrier distribution of $^{32}\mathrm{S} + ^{96}\mathrm{Zr}$ was broaden and extended to lower energy than in the case of $^{32}\mathrm{S} + ^{96}\mathrm{Zr}$ due to the coupling of neutron transfer with positive $Q$ -values, which resulted in a significant enhancement of fusion cross sections at the subbarrier energies.

# 3 Nuclear Astrophysics

The measurement of $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{p})^9\mathrm{Li}$ was used to deduce the $S$ -factors of $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{n},\gamma)^9\mathrm{Li}$ and $^8\mathrm{B}(\mathrm{p},\gamma)^9\mathrm{C}$ reactions by ANC method. The reaction of $^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{n})^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ was measured. Also, the $^{10}\mathrm{C}$ secondary beam was produced. A setup for resonant scattering reactions with thick target was established.

# 4 Nuclear Data Measurement

We finished the measurement of double differential cross sections of $^{6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ induced by fast neutrons. And the methodology study for neutron resonance parameter measurement within 0.1-1.0 MeV was made by using HI-13 tandem accelerator facility. Research on EUV spectra of highly ionized titanium and lifetime measurement of highly ionized ions were conducted.

# 5 Application of Nuclear Technology

By using accelerator mass spectrometry, traces of ${}^{129}\mathrm{I}$ , ${}^{99}\mathrm{Tc}$ , ${}^{41}\mathrm{Ca}$ , ${}^{151}\mathrm{Sm}$ and ${}^{79}\mathrm{Se}$ were precisely measured. Radiation effects in various biological molecules and cells were investigated with ${}^{7}\mathrm{Li}$ ion and protons beams. The single-event effects were calibrated for some important detectors and CMOS SRAM. Some properties of solid material surface modified by nuclear tracks were studied.

# 6 Theoretical Nuclear Physics

Calculations of electromagnetic transition probabilities and energy levels of the doublet bands in $^{134}\mathrm{Pr}$ were carried out with triaxial projected shell model and lead to a conclusion that the observed nearly degenerate doublet bands in $^{134}\mathrm{Pr}$ can not be interpreted as chiral bands. Relativistic microscopic optical potential of the nucleon scattering off nuclei was investigated in the framework of the Dirac Brueckner-Hartree-Fock approach. A special attention was paid to the isospin dependence of the nucleon effective interaction and effective mass. The dynamic calculation of heavy ion fusion was applied to study of super heavy element.

# 7 Academic Exchange

The academic exchange has been enhanced year by year. The tandem beam time about 2000 hours was provided to outside users such as Peking University, Tsinghua University, Jilin University and Institute of Modern Physics etc. For all users, the publication of SCI papers was about 50 per year.

The Tandem Upgrading Project, or Beijing Radioactive Ion-beam Facility (BRIF), was approved. A construction contract was signed to build a 100 MeV, $200\mu \mathrm{A}$ compact proton cyclotron. The cyclotron design started in this year.

Finally, I would like to thank all of scientists, technicians and administration stuffs for their great contribution to the progress of this laboratory.

Director

Department of Nuclear Physics, CIAE December 6, 2006

# 1 Misleading Chiral Bands in $^{134}\mathrm{Pr}^*$

Chen Yongshou, Gao Zaochun

(China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-18, Beijing 102413, China)

The chirality is common and has important consequence in science. Many biological and pharmaceutical molecules have static chirality when they are composed of four different atoms. The chirality may exist in nuclei when a particular angular momentum coupling scheme appears, where three angular momenta of the valence neutrons, the valence protons and the core are mutually perpendicular so that a left- and a right-handed systems can be formed. The possibility was considered recently, following the observation of doublet bands built on the intrinsic configuration of $\pi h_{11/2} \otimes \nu h_{11/2}$ in the $A = 130$ mass region. $^{134}$ Pr is known as a best candidate where the nearly degenerate doublet bands were found experimentally. The energy degeneracy of doublet band states with the same spin and parity is an important indication for the occurrence of chirality. Among other things, however, the most crucial criteria for the chirality are the equivalence of the reduced E2 transition probabilities in the doublet bands. Very recently, the decisive experiment of measuring electromagnetic transition rates have been done, and a large difference of the experimental $B(\mathrm{E}2)$ values for the doublet bands of ${}^{134}\mathrm{Pr}$ has been reported $^{[1]}$ . Then a serious question arises whether the observed doublet bands in ${}^{134}\mathrm{Pr}$ can be characterized as having chirality, and this is the issue that underlies the present investigation. To answer this question we carried out calculations of electromagnetic transition probabilities and energy levels of the doublet bands with triaxial projected shell model (TPSM) which has been recently developed in CIAE.

The shell model Hamiltonian, involving a large number of nucleons moving in a spherical Nilsson potential and an interaction of separable multipole $Q \cdot Q$ forces plus monopole pairing plus quadrupole pairing, is written as

$$
H = H _ {0} - \frac {1}{2} \sum_ {\lambda = 2} ^ {4} \chi_ {\lambda} \sum_ {\mu = - \lambda} ^ {\lambda} Q _ {\lambda \mu} ^ {+} Q _ {\lambda \mu} - G _ {0} P _ {0 0} ^ {+} P _ {0 0} - G _ {2} \sum_ {\mu = - 2} ^ {2} P _ {2 \mu} ^ {+} P _ {2 \mu} \tag {1}
$$

The wave function is given by

$$
\left| \Psi_ {\mathrm {I M}} ^ {\sigma} \right\rangle = \sum_ {K \kappa} f _ {\mathrm {I K} \kappa} ^ {\sigma} \hat {P} _ {\mathrm {M K}} ^ {I} | \phi_ {\kappa} \rangle \tag {2}
$$

where the three-dimensional angular momentum projection operator is written as

$$
P _ {\mathrm {M K}} ^ {I} = \frac {2 I + 1}{8 \pi^ {2}} \int \mathrm {d} \Omega D _ {\mathrm {M K}} ^ {I} (\Omega) \widehat {R} (\Omega) \tag {3}
$$

and $|\phi_{\kappa}\rangle$ represents a set of triaxially deformed BCS multi-quasiparticle states. By solving the Hamiltonian equation

$$
H \left| \Psi_ {\mathrm {I M}} ^ {\sigma} \right\rangle = E ^ {\sigma} (I) \left| \Psi_ {\mathrm {I M}} ^ {\sigma} \right\rangle \tag {4}
$$

we obtain energies $E^{\sigma}(I)$ of states " $\sigma$ " as a function of spin $I$ , together with wave functions $f_{\mathbb{K}\kappa}^{\sigma}$ . The

reduced rate of electromagnetic transitions between the eigenstates $|\Psi_{\mathrm{IM}}^{\sigma}\rangle$ , induced by a spherical tensor operator $\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{\lambda \mu}$ , is

$$
B (\mathbf {M} \lambda ; i \rightarrow f) = \frac {2 I _ {f} + 1}{2 I _ {i} + 1} \left|\left\langle \Psi_ {I _ {f}} ^ {\sigma_ {f} \pi_ {f}} \left\| \hat {\mathbf {M}} _ {\lambda} \right\| \Psi_ {I _ {i}} ^ {\sigma_ {i} \pi_ {i}} \right\rangle\right| ^ {2} \tag {5}
$$

and the reduced matrix elements become

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\langle \Psi_ {I _ {f}} ^ {\sigma_ {f} \pi_ {f}} \left\| \widehat {\mathbf {M}} _ {\lambda} \right\| \Psi_ {I _ {i}} ^ {\sigma_ {i} \pi_ {i}} \right\rangle = \frac {1}{2} (1 + \pi_ {f} \pi_ {i} \pi_ {\lambda}) \sum_ {K _ {i} \kappa_ {i} K _ {f} \kappa_ {f}} f _ {I _ {f} K _ {f} \kappa_ {f}} ^ {\sigma_ {f} \pi_ {f}} f _ {I _ {i} K _ {i} \kappa_ {i}} ^ {\sigma_ {i} \pi_ {i}} \\ \sum_ {\mu} \left\langle I _ {i} K _ {f} - \mu \lambda \mu \mid I _ {f} K _ {f} \right\rangle \left\langle \phi_ {\kappa_ {f}} \left| \hat {\mathbf {M}} _ {\lambda \mu} P _ {K _ {f} - \mu K _ {i}} ^ {I _ {i} \pi_ {i}} \right| \phi_ {\kappa_ {i}} \right\rangle \tag {6} \\ \end{array}
$$

The calculated band energies are compared with experimental data and shown in Fig.1, and it is seen that a good agreement between theory and experiment has been achieved. Particularly, the crossing behavior of the band 1 (yrast) and the band 2 (nonyrast) was reproduced by the present calculation. At the nearly degenerate region, from $I = 14$ to 17, calculated $B(\mathrm{E}2)$ values are larger for band 1 than for band 2 by a factor of 3, in average, see Fig.2, and this is in a nice agreement with the experimental $B(\mathrm{E}2)$ data<sup>[1]</sup>. Detailed analysis for the intrinsic structures of calculated doublet bands indicates that from spin $I = 14$ to 18 the doublet bands have completely different nature, namely the band 1 is built on a 2 quasiparticle (q.p.) state, 1n1p, while the band 2 is mainly built on a 4 q.p. one, 1n3p. The shell model configuration mixing of 4 q.p. component gives rise to a strong reduction of $B(\mathrm{E}2)$ values of the band 2 in the band interaction region, $I = 14$ to 17. The chiral doublet bands should have a similar intrinsic structure, therefore our theoretical results lead to a conclusion that the observed nearly degenerate doublet bands in $^{134}\mathrm{Pr}$ can not be interpreted as chiral bands.

![](images/adec57632377c28c33fa2b02c05015369283031fbcf9e65b1db79719849d9545.jpg)  
Fig. 1 Calculated and experimental energy levels of the chirality candidate bands of $^{134}\mathrm{Pr}$

![](images/644e6e41ff76207974fcae79b1bc631a163f6055918ae687d2f868af78c4685d.jpg)  
Fig. 2 Calculated $E2$ transition probabilities of the chirality candidate bands of $^{134}\mathrm{Pr}$

# 中文摘要

原子核中也可能存在手征性， ${}^{134}\mathrm{Pr}$ 被认为是最好的候选者，因为在此核中实验上发现了近似简并的二重带。然而，手征性最关键的判据并不是能量的简并，而是二重带的带内E2跃迁概率要基本相同。新近的实验测量表明，此二重带的B(E2)实验值与报报道值很不相同。这样， ${}^{134}\mathrm{Pr}$ 二重带的手征性质就成了大问题。为了回答这个问题，我们发展三轴投影壳模型，计算了此二重带的电磁跃迁概率和能级，计算结果很好地再现了实验数据。深入分析表明，在 $I = 14$ 到18区间，二重带具有完全不同的性质，因此， ${}^{134}\mathrm{Pr}$ 二重带不能解释为手征带。

# 2 Signature Inversion as a Probe for Triaxial Rotation*

Chen Yongshou, Gao Zaochun

(China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-18, Beijing 102413, China)

The signature inversion phenomenon has been widely observed in nuclear rotational spectra but has no common understanding for more than 20 years. The signature inversion is strongly related to the triaxial rotation, while how a triaxially deformed nucleus rotates is a fundamental question in nuclear structure. The present study attempts to achieve a thorough explanation for the signature inversion and to

provide the rules to probe the rotation of triaxial nuclei by examining experimental signature inversion data. The signature is associated with the invariance of a system with intrinsic quadrupole deformation under a rotation of $180^{\circ}$ around a principal axis, and is defined in the cranking model. We define the dynamic cranking axis as the axis along which the total angular momentum has a largest component. Note that the dynamic cranking axis can be any one of three principal axes of a rotating triaxial nucleus, while the cranking axis defined in the cranking model is a fixed one. We interpret the signature inversion as the change of the dynamic cranking axis in the rotational triaxial nuclear system. Take the yrast band of old proton nucleus $^{157}\mathrm{Ho}$ as an example, where the twice signature inversions were observed experimentally at spin $I = 37 / 2$ and $53 / 2$ respectively[1]. This band is based on an intrinsic configuration of the Fermi aligned proton orbital in the proton $h_{11 / 2}$ shell, namely the proton Fermi level lies in the middle of the shell, which has a large alignment component $I_{y}$ along the y-axis, the intermediate axis, thus the occurrence of the signature inversion in the band is mainly determined by the characteristic of the y-axis, namely as the dynamic cranking axis or not. In order to investigate the double signature inversion and explore the triaxial rotation the quantity $S(I) = E(I) - E(I - 1)$ for the yrast band of $^{157}\mathrm{Ho}$ was calculated with the triaxial projected shell model (TPSM) which has recently been developed in CIAE as an extension of RASM. The shell model Hamiltonian, involving a large number of nucleons moving in a spherical Nilsson potential and an interaction of separable multipole $\mathcal{Q} \cdot \mathcal{Q}$ forces plus monopole pairing plus quadrupole pairing, is written as

$$
H = H _ {0} - \frac {1}{2} \sum_ {\lambda = 2} ^ {4} \chi_ {\lambda} \sum_ {\mu = - \lambda} ^ {\lambda} Q _ {\lambda \mu} ^ {+} Q _ {\lambda \mu} - G _ {0} P _ {0 0} ^ {+} P _ {0 0} - G _ {2} \sum_ {\mu = - 2} ^ {2} P _ {2 \mu} ^ {+} P _ {2 \mu} \tag {1}
$$

The wave function is given by

$$
\left| \Psi_ {\mathrm {I M}} ^ {\sigma} \right\rangle = \sum_ {K \kappa} f _ {\mathrm {I K} \kappa} ^ {\sigma} \hat {P} _ {\mathrm {M K}} ^ {I} \left| \phi_ {\kappa} \right\rangle \tag {2}
$$

where the three-dimensional angular momentum projection operator is written as

$$
P _ {\mathrm {M K}} ^ {I} = \frac {2 I + 1}{8 \pi^ {2}} \int D _ {\mathrm {M K}} ^ {I} (\Omega) \hat {R} (\Omega) \mathrm {d} \Omega \tag {3}
$$

and $|\phi_{\kappa}\rangle$ represents a set of triaxially deformed BCS multi-quasiparticle states. By solving the Hamiltonian equation

$$
H \left| \Psi_ {\mathrm {I M}} ^ {\sigma} \right\rangle = E ^ {\sigma} (I) \left| \Psi_ {\mathrm {I M}} ^ {\sigma} \right\rangle \tag {4}
$$

We obtain energies $E^{\sigma}(I)$ of states " $\sigma$ " as a function of spin $I$ , together with wave functions $f_{\mathrm{IK}\kappa}^{\sigma}$ . The expectation values of the components of total angular momentum along the three intrinsic principal axis can be calculated by using the eigenstate wave function $\left|\Psi_{\mathrm{IM}}^{\sigma}\right\rangle$ . The calculated results quite well reproduce the double signature inversions observed in $^{157}\mathrm{Ho}$ , as shown in Fig.1. Detailed calculations show that the first signature inversion is caused by the change of the dynamic cranking axis from the $y-$ to the $x$ -axis, while the second inversion is due to the change back of the dynamic cranking axis to the $y$ -axis from the $x$ -axis, the shortest axis (Fig.2). The cause of the dynamic axis change may be attributed to the alignments of the pair of neutrons in the $i_{13/2}$ shell. The mechanism for signature inversion provided in the

present study is general, can be applied to, for example, odd-odd nuclei $^{120 - 130}\mathrm{Cs}$ , where the dynamic cranking axis is the $x$ -axis at low spins and becomes the $y$ -axis after the inversion spin[2].

![](images/cec41f0c355cc335a70dcf6a10210589249629e27f782fe81bb5d003635082f6.jpg)  
Fig. 1 Calculated energies $E(I) - E(I - 1)$ of the yrast band states for $^{157}\mathrm{Ho}$ ,

compared with the experimental data

Arrows indicate the signature reversion point

![](images/66fad4d50ff19f610829850aaadb17433c4889c8efe721774e49c9aa63270b6f.jpg)  
Fig. 2 Calculated expectation values of $I_x^2, I_y^2$ and $I_z^2$ with the wave functions that has reproduced the double signature inversions in Fig.1

[2] Gao Zaochun, Chen Yongshou, Yang Sun. Phys Lett, 2006, B634: 195.

# 中文摘要

二十多年来，旋称反转现象在核转动谱中被大量地观测到，但没有统一的认识。本研究试图对旋称反转给出完全的解释，并提出由旋称反转实验数据出发分析三轴形变核转动的规律。我们定义动力学推转轴为总角动量在其上有最大分量之轴，我们解释旋称反转为三轴转动核中动力学推转轴的改变。

# 3 Theoretical Study on the ${}^{2}\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{d},\gamma)^{4}\mathrm{He}$ Reaction Below $E_{\mathrm{c.m.}} \leqslant 3$ MeV

Ma Yinqun $^{1,2}$ , Ma Zhongyu $^{1}$

(1. China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-18, Beijing 102413, China;   
2. Physics Department, Taiyuan Normal University, Taiyuan 030001, China)

The quantitative Cosmology is becoming a valid description of modern models of the early universe. The results deduced from the prediction of big-bang nucleosynthesis (BBN), combined with the latest Cosmic Microwave Background anisotropy measurements, give independent and accurate predictions of the baryon density and can be used to test the standard cosmology framework. The abundances of the light nuclei, ${}^{2}\mathrm{H}, {}^{3}\mathrm{He}, {}^{4}\mathrm{He}$ and ${}^{7}\mathrm{Li}$ have been used to test the consistency of the hot big-bang model at early (first $10^{3}$ seconds) stages of evolution of the universe. To construct the BBN reaction network, the astronomical measurements of the light element abundances need to be complemented by the reaction rate knowledge in the relevant energy region (from few tens to few hundreds of keV). The deuteron- deuteron radiative capture reaction is one of the deuterium-burning processes for which the cross section is not well known at very low energies. For the reaction ${}^{2}\mathrm{H}(\mathrm{d},\gamma)^{4}\mathrm{He}$ due to extremely low reaction rates at the astrophysically important energies (about below few hundreds of keV), the relevant cross section data are impractically obtained for direct laboratory measurements. The present study was undertaken to obtain ones.

We present a phenomenological study of the ${}^{2}\mathrm{H}(\mathrm{d},\gamma)^{4}\mathrm{He}$ reaction that allows for the D-state component of the colliding deuterons. In the L-S coupling states of the d-d system may have the following possible states with lowest angular momenta: ${}^{3}S_{0},{}^{5}S_{2},{}^{3}P_{0,1,2},{}^{1}D_{2}$ . Considering electromagnetic radiation with multipolarity less than 2, the following transitions are allowed:

$$
\left\langle^ {4} \mathrm {H e} | \mathrm {E} 2 | ^ {5} S _ {2} \right\rangle , \left\langle^ {4} \mathrm {H e} | \mathrm {E} 2 | ^ {1} D _ {2} \right\rangle
$$

The wave function of the d-d system can be expressed as produces of internal deuteron wave functions $\Phi_d^S$ and a relative motion $f(R)$ ,

$$
\psi \left(^ {2 S + 1} L _ {J}\right) = \frac {f _ {L S} ^ {J} (R)}{R} \left[ Y _ {L} (\hat {R}) \left(\Phi_ {d} ^ {S _ {1} = 1} \Phi_ {d} ^ {S = 1}\right) _ {S} \right] _ {J} \tag {1}
$$

Considering D-state components in $^4\mathrm{He}$ , the ground state of $^4\mathrm{He}$ could be expressed as

$$
\psi_ {\alpha} = \cos \omega \psi \left(^ {1} S _ {0}\right) + \sin \omega \psi \left(^ {5} D _ {0}\right) \tag {2}
$$

where the radial wave functions are normalized to one. The mixing angular $\omega$ parameterizes the D-state amplitude in $^4\mathrm{He}$ . The scattering wave functions of the d-d system are,

$$
\psi = i ^ {L} \left(\frac {4 \pi (2 L + 1)}{v}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \frac {1}{k} \psi \left(^ {2 S + 1} L _ {J}\right) \tag {3}
$$

where $L$ is the orbital angular momentum, $S$ the channel spin, and $J$ the total angular momentum. $\nu$ and $k$ are relative velocity and wave number of the colliding deuterons. The scattering wave functions are normalized to unit flux.

We simply display our results for the capture cross section

$$
\sigma \left(E _ {\mathrm {c . m .}}\right) = \sum_ {S = 0, 2} \frac {4 \pi}{7 5} \frac {2 S + 1}{4 5} \left(\frac {E _ {\gamma}}{\hbar \nu}\right) ^ {5} \frac {1}{k ^ {2}} \frac {e ^ {2}}{\hbar \nu} \left| A \left(^ {2 S + 1} L _ {J}\right) \right| ^ {2} \tag {4}
$$

where $E_{\gamma} = E_{\mathrm{c.m.}} + 23.847 \, \mathrm{MeV}$ is the energy of the emitted photon, $A(^{2S+1}L_J)$ is the transition amplitude. As mentioned above, we will adjust $\omega$ to reproduce the absolute normalization of the low energy experimental cross section. At low energies, it is most convenient to present the data in terms of the astrophysical S-factor, defined by

$$
S \left(E _ {\mathrm {c . m .}}\right) = \sigma \left(E _ {\mathrm {c . m .}}\right) E _ {\mathrm {c . m .}} \exp (2 \pi \eta) \tag {5}
$$

where $\eta = e^2 /\hbar \nu$ is the Summerfield parameter. The radial wave functions in $^4\mathrm{He}$ are calculated in WoodsSaxon potentials, which parameters are adjusted to reproduce the d-d break up energies of $^4\mathrm{He}$ and low energy d-d elastic phase shifts, respectively. The parameters in our calculations are given in table 1. We can well reproduce the energy dependence of the $S$ -factor if we choose $\omega = -0.275$ for the potential leading to a $^4\mathrm{He}$ D-state probability of $P_{\mathrm{D}} = 7.4\%$ , and theoretical results are compared with the experimental data in Fig.1. The agreement with the experimental data at $E_{\mathrm{c.m.}}\leqslant 3$ MeV is very satisfactory. We extend our model to the energy range $E_{\mathrm{c.m.}}\leqslant 50$ keV, which is important for the $^4\mathrm{He}$ nucleosynthesis during the big bang. Our $S$ -factor is nearly linear in energy: $S(E_{\mathrm{c.m.}})\approx S_0 + S_1E_{\mathrm{c.m.}}$ and can be well approximated with the coefficients $S_0 = 6.29\times 10^{-6}\mathrm{keV}\cdot \mathrm{b}$ and $S_{1} = -1.14\times 10^{-8}\mathrm{b}$

Table 1 Woods-Saxon parameters used in theoretical calculations

![](images/081df9ac42c2b057bbde093a3cdf44228c2e379dbef9a41b9ee74b9125b8c92b.jpg)

# References

[1] Descouvement P, et al. Nucl Phys, 2005, A758: 783c.   
[2] Pierarcwicz J, et al. Phys Rev, 1987, C36: 875.   
[3] Whitton R M, et al. Phys Rev, 1993, C48: 2355.   
[4] Sabourov K, et al. Phys Rev, 2004, C70: 064601-1.

# 中文摘要

在唯象模型的基础上，考虑到碰撞氘核的D态分量对跃迁矩阵元的贡献和 ${}^{4}\mathrm{He}$ 基态D态成分，从理论上计算了低能 ${}^{2}\mathrm{H}(\mathrm{d},\gamma)^{4}\mathrm{He}$ 反应的天体物理学S因子。由理论计算再现d-d系统的结合能以及由共振群方法计算的d-d弹性散射相移，得到Woods-Saxon势参数。并由此计算的S因子与实验数据在 $E < 3\mathrm{MeV}$ 范围符合得较好，理论计算结果与实验数据符合很好。由理论计算结果外推，计算了目前还没有实验数据的使恒星核合成反应过程能够进行的能区(1～20keV）的S因子。

# 4 Nucleon Effective Mass in Dirac Brueckner-Hartree-Fock Approach*

Ma Zhongyu<sup>1</sup>, Rong Jian<sup>1</sup>, Chen Baoqiu<sup>1</sup>, Zhu Zhiyuan<sup>2</sup>, Song Hongqiu<sup>2</sup>

(1. China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-18, Beijing 102413, China;

2. Shanghai Institute of Applied Physics, Chinese Academy of Sciences, Shanghai 201800, China)

The nucleon effective mass characterizes the propagation of a nucleon in the nuclear medium, which is adopted to describe an independent quasi-particle model in the nuclear many-body system[1,2]. One of the most interesting questions is the isospin dependence of the nucleon effective interaction as well as the nucleon effective mass. The knowledge about the isospin dependence of the nucleon effective mass is

critically important for understanding properties of neutron stars and the dynamics of nuclear collisions induced by radioactive beams[3-5]. Unfortunately, up to now the knowledge about the isospin dependence of those quantities from experiments is very little. Recently, $\mathrm{Li}^{[6]}$ studied the constraint of the neutron-proton effective mass splitting in the neutron-rich nuclear matter. He found that an effective mass splitting of $m_{\mathrm{n}}^{*} < m_{\mathrm{p}}^{*}$ leads to a symmetric potential that is inconsistent with the energy dependence of the Lane potential constrained by the nucleon-nucleus scattering experimental data. The purpose of this work is to study the isospin dependence of the nucleon effective mass microscopically in a relativistic approach. We shall first clarify the definition of the nucleon effective mass in the relativistic approach and, then investigate the isospin dependence of the nucleon effective mass in the Dirac Brueckner-Hartree-Fock (DBHF) approach.

In the non-relativistic approach the nuclear microscopic potential $V(k,\varepsilon)$ is nonlocal and of frequency dependence. The effective mass represents the non-locality of the microscopic nuclear potential. The nucleon effective mass can be derived by following two equivalent expressions[7],

$$
\frac {M ^ {*}}{M} = 1 - \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} \varepsilon} V (k (\varepsilon), \varepsilon) = \left(1 + \frac {M}{k} \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} k} V (k, \varepsilon (k))\right) \tag {1}
$$

where $\varepsilon(k)$ is a function of the momentum $k$ defined by the energy momentum relation, $\varepsilon = k^2 / 2M + V(k,\varepsilon)$ . The nucleon effective mass can be determined from the analysis of the experimental data performed in the framework of the nonrelativistic shell and optical models. The typical value of the nucleon effective mass is $M^{*} / M \approx 0.70 \pm 0.05$ , at $\varepsilon \approx 30 \mathrm{MeV}$ , which is obtained by a large body of phenomenological nonrelativistic analyses of experimental scattering data.

Recently developed relativistic mean field (RMF) approach is very successful in the description of nuclear ground state properties[8], which becomes a very popular tool in the study of the nuclear structure and nuclear reactions. In the relativistic approach, the nucleon effective mass is always addressed in such a way that the dressed nucleon in the nuclear medium could be described by a Dirac equation. In the Dirac equation the nucleon effective mass is largely reduced by the nucleon attractive scalar potential in the nuclear medium. $M_{\mathrm{s}}^{*} = M + U_{\mathrm{s}}$ , where $U_{\mathrm{s}}$ is the nuclear scalar potential. We may call this nucleon effective mass the scalar effective mass or Dirac mass[9]. The nuclear scalar potential in the RMF is about $U_{\mathrm{s}} = -(375 \pm 40) \, \mathrm{MeV}$ , which corresponds to $M_{\mathrm{s}}^{*} / M = 0.60 \pm 0.04$ [8]. However, this definition of the effective mass is not directly related to the same empirical value derived from the analysis of experimental data in the non-relativistic shell and optical models, as described above. Therefore this effective mass denotes different physical quantities in comparison with that in Eq.(1). This confusion has already been pointed out and clarified by Jaminon and Mahaux[7] many year ago. In order to compare a same quantity in the non-relativistic and relativistic approaches one derives a Schroedinger equivalent equation for the upper component of the nucleon Dirac spinor by eliminating the lower component. The Schroedinger equivalent central potential could be expressed as $V_{\mathrm{cen}} = (U_{\mathrm{s}}^{2} - U_{0}^{2} + 2EU_{0} + 2MU_{\mathrm{s}}) / 2M$ , where $U_{0}$ is the nucleon vector potential[8] and $E = \varepsilon + M$ is the nucleon energy in the relativistic approach. The Schroedinger equivalent central potential plays the same role as the potential $V(\varepsilon)$ in the non-relativistic approach. Therefore, the non-relativistic-type effective mass as defined in Eq.(1) can be derived in a similar way[7]. In the RMF the nucleon scalar and vector potentials are energy independent, which yields $M_{\mathrm{v}}^{*} / M =$

$1 - U_{0} / M$ . This effective mass may be called the vector effective mass of the nucleon or Lorentz mass[7], which characterizes the energy dependence of the Schroedinger equivalent central potential. In the RMF theory the isospin dependence of the nucleon self-energy is obtained through the exchange of the isovector meson $\rho$ , which produces a stronger vector potential for neutron than that for proton in the neutron-rich nuclear matter and keeps same scalar potentials for neutron and proton. Therefore in the RMF one obtains $M_{\mathrm{s}}^{*}(N) = M_{\mathrm{s}}^{*}(P)$ and $M_{\mathrm{v}}^{*}(N) < M_{\mathrm{v}}^{*}(P)$ in the neutron-rich nuclear matter. If one introduces the isovector scalar meson $\delta$ in the RMF, which produces a more attractive scalar potential for neutron, even the neutron scalar effective mass is smaller than that of proton in the neutron-rich nuclear matter. However, it is well known that the microscopic nuclear scalar and vector potentials are of the momentum and energy dependence. Taking account of the energy dependence of the nucleon scalar and vector potentials, the vector effective mass of a nucleon is now obtained as follows,

$$
M _ {\mathrm {v}} ^ {*} / M = 1 - U _ {0} / M - \left(1 + U _ {\mathrm {s}} / M\right) \mathrm {d} U _ {s} / \mathrm {d} \varepsilon - \left(1 + \varepsilon / M - U _ {0} / M\right) \mathrm {d} U _ {0} / \mathrm {d} \varepsilon \tag {2}
$$

In order to consider the energy dependence of the nucleon self-energy in the nuclear medium we shall study microscopically the nucleon scalar and vector potentials in an asymmetric nuclear matter (ASNM) in the DBHF approach. In this work we adopt a new decomposition of the Dirac structure of nucleon self-energies in the DBHF proposed by Schiller and Mäuther[10,11]. The DBHF G matrix was separated into a bare nucleon-nucleon (NN) interaction $V$ and a correlation term $\Delta G$ . The bare NN interaction is described by meson exchanges, such as one boson exchange potentials (OBEP). The coupling constants and meson masses are determined by experimental phase shifts of the NN scattering and the ground state properties of the deuteron, which are not changed in the DBHF approach. A projection method is applied only on the correlation term $\Delta G$ and it is parameterized by four pseudomesons. The effective NN interaction $G$ in the DBHF in the symmetric nuclear matter (SNM) and the ASNM can be characterized by the exchanges of those mesons in the relativistic Hratree-Fock approach.

The nucleon self-energy in nuclear matter in the DBHF approach can be calculated with a bare NN potential $V$ and parameterized $\Delta G$ .

$$
\Sigma (k) = \sum_ {\alpha} \int \frac {\mathrm {d} ^ {4} q}{(2 \pi) ^ {4}} \left\{\Gamma_ {\alpha} ^ {a} \Delta_ {\alpha} ^ {a b} (0) \operatorname {T r} \left[ \mathrm {i} \Gamma_ {\alpha} ^ {b} G (q) \right] - \mathrm {i} \Gamma_ {\alpha} ^ {a} \Delta_ {\alpha} ^ {a b} (q) \Gamma_ {\alpha} ^ {b} G (k - q) \right\} \tag {3}
$$

The index $\alpha$ refers to mesons, $a$ and $b$ refer to isospin components. $G$ is the single particle Green's function, and $\Delta_{\alpha}^{ab}$ are meson propagators. The vertex $\Gamma_{\alpha}^{a}$ is expressed as: $i_g$ for scalar mesons, $-g\gamma^\mu$ for vector mesons and $-f\gamma^5\gamma^\mu q_\mu /m$ for pseudoscalar mesons, respectively and multiplied by an isospin operator $\Gamma_{\alpha}$ for isovector mesons. Due to the rotational invariance in the infinite nuclear matter, the self-energy of nucleon can be written as:

$$
\Sigma^ {i} (k) = \Sigma_ {\mathrm {s}} ^ {i} (k) - \gamma_ {0} \Sigma_ {0} ^ {i} (k) + \gamma k i \Sigma_ {\mathrm {v}} ^ {i} (k) \tag {4}
$$

Where $i$ refers to proton and neutron. The Dirac equation of a nucleon in the nuclear medium has the form,

$$
[ \alpha p + \gamma_ {0} (M + U _ {\mathrm {s}} ^ {i}) + U _ {0} ^ {i} ] \psi i (r) = \varepsilon^ {i} \psi i (r) \tag {5}
$$

where $U_{s}^{i}$ and $U_{0}^{i}$ are scalar and vector potential, respectively, which are modified by the space-like

vector potential $\Sigma_{\mathbf{v}}^{i}$

$$
U _ {\mathrm {s}} ^ {i} = \frac {\Sigma_ {\mathrm {s}} ^ {i} - M \Sigma_ {\mathrm {v}} ^ {i}}{1 + \Sigma_ {\mathrm {v}} ^ {i}}, \quad U _ {0} ^ {i} = \frac {- \Sigma_ {0} ^ {i} + \varepsilon^ {i} \Sigma_ {i} ^ {\mathrm {v}}}{1 + \Sigma_ {i} ^ {\mathrm {v}}} \tag {6}
$$

In the SNM, it is found that the nucleon scalar effective mass, $M^{*} / M = 0.64$ at the Fermi surface and the vector effective mass $M^{*} / M = 0.73$ , if one neglects the energy dependence of the nucleon self-energy. Both nucleon scalar and vector potentials decrease as the nucleon energy increases. In the ASNM, one defines an asymmetric parameter $\beta = (\rho_{\mathrm{n}} - \rho_{\mathrm{p}}) = (\rho_{\mathrm{n}} + \rho_{\mathrm{p}})$ , where $\rho_{\mathrm{n}}$ and $\rho_{\mathrm{p}}$ denote densities of neutron and proton, respectively. In the neutron-rich nuclear matter the neutron self-energy, both scalar and vector potentials become stronger than those of proton due to the larger neutron density, while the difference of neutron and proton vector potentials is much larger than that of scalar potentials. Therefore the phenomenological coupling constant of the isovector-scalar meson $\delta$ is usually taken to be rather weak or simply omitted in the RMF. Although the energy dependence of nucleon potentials is not strong, the energy dependence of neutron scalar and vector potentials is steeper than those of proton, especially for the vector potential.

![](images/046dc9826aff43e7c822c3870bf9203b6d852710de6e7d961e916fb089b50a9d.jpg)  
Fig. 1 Nucleon scalar and vector effective masses at $E = 50$ MeV in the asymmetric nuclear matter with $k_{\mathrm{F}} = 1.36$ fm

Nucleon effective masses calculated in the DBHF nucleon potentials are shown in Fig.1, where upper and lower curves correspond to nucleon scalar and vector effective masses, respectively. The solid (dashed) curves are those of neutron (proton). Obviously, the neutron scalar mass is smaller than that of proton in the neutron-rich nuclear matter due to the stronger scalar potential of neutron. However, the difference of neutron and proton scalar effective masses is very small in the neutron-rich nuclear matter. It is found that the energy dependence of the nucleon self-energy largely reduces the nucleon vector effective mass. At the nucleon energy $\varepsilon = 50\mathrm{MeV}$ , the nucleon scalar and vector effective masses in the SNM are 0.66 and 0.68, respectively. Due to the stronger energy dependence of the neutron vector

potential the neutron vector effective mass becomes larger than that of proton in the neutron-rich nuclear matter. This result is consistent with the microscopic calculations in the non-relativistic approach, e.g., the Landau-Fermi liquid theory[17] and the BHF approach[18] as well as the energy and isospin dependence of the nucleon-nucleus scattering experimental data[6].

In summary, we have clarified the definition of nucleon scalar and vector effective masses in the relativistic approach, where only the nucleon vector effective mass could be compared with that in the non-relativistic approach. The nucleon effective mass defined in the non-relativistic approach could be compared with the empirical value extracted from the analysis of the shell and optical models. The isospin dependence of the nucleon vector effective mass is calculated in the DBHF in the ASNM. It is found that the neutron vector effective mass is larger than that of proton in the neutron-rich nuclear matter if the energy dependence of the nucleon self-energy is taken into account. This result is consistent with what was found in the microscopic studies of non-relativistic approach.

# 中文摘要

文章在Dirac Brueckner-Hartree-Fock (DBHF)理论框架下研究了核子的有效质量。在相对论描述中可以定义核子的标量和矢量有效质量，而非相对论描述中的核子有效质量是从壳模型和光学势来得到的，文章指出相对论描述中核子矢量有效质量是与非相对论描述中的有效质量可以相比较的物理量。相对论描述中核子在核介质中的势场的能量依赖性是非常重要的，只有考虑了核子势场的能量依赖性才能得到合理的核子有效质量的同位旋相关性。在DBHF计算中丰中子物质中中子的矢量有效质量大于质子的矢量有效质量，这个结果与唯象光学势的分析结果一致。

# 5 Pigmy Dipole Mode in $^{26,28}$ Ne in the Quasiparticle Relativistic Random Phase Approximation*

Cao Ligang $^{1}$ , Ma Zhongyu $^{2}$

(1. Institute of High Energy Physics, Chinese Academy of Sciences, Beijing 100049, China;

2. China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-18, Beijing 102413, China)

Recently, Gibelin et al.[1,2] have measured the inelastic scattering of $^{26}\mathrm{Ne} + ^{208}\mathrm{Pb}$ using a 60 MeV/u $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ secondary beam at RIKEN. This reaction is dominated by Coulomb excitations and selective for $E1$ transitions. Low-lying electric dipole modes may appear in weakly bound nuclei, which are so-called Pygmy Dipole Resonances. Although carrying only a small fraction of the full dipole strength these states are of particular interest because they are expected to reflect the motion of the neutron skin against the core formed with an equal number of protons and neutrons. The increase of the dipole strength at low energies in neutron-rich nuclei could affect the corresponding radiative neutron capture cross section considerably, which has a significance in astrophysics. Therefore the theoretical investigation of Pigmy dipole resonances (PDR) have been paid much attention. Study the PDR in $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ has a practical significance. In this work, we investigate the properties of low-lying dipole modes in neutron rich nuclei $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ in the relativistic approach which is formulated in the response function method. $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ are neutron rich nuclei, whose Fermi surfaces are close to the particle continuum. Therefore the description of those nuclei has to explicitly include the coupling between bound states and the particle continuum. The quasiparticle relativistic random phase approximation (QRRPA) is adopted, which is an extension of the fully consistent RRPA[3-5] by taking into account the effect of pairing correlations. The ground state properties of neutron rich nuclei $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ are calculated in the extended relativistic mean field and Bardeen-Cooper-Schrieffer (RMF+BCS) approxiamtion[6], where the resonant continuum is properly treated. The empirical pairing gaps deduced from odd-even mass differences are adopted in the BCS calculation in this work. All calculations are performed with the parameter set NL3[7], which gives a good description of not only the ground state properties[8] but also the collective giant resonance[3-5,9-11].

In Fig.1 we present the Hartree and perturbed strengths for the isovector dipole mode in nuclei $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ (upper panel) and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ (lower panel) calculated in the RRPA and QRRPA approaches. Short-dashed

curves represent the RRPA strengths, the QRRPA response functions are denoted by solid ones. It can be seen that the perturbed strengths at the energy above $10\mathrm{MeV}$ for those neutron rich nuclei are very fragmented. Compared to the Hartree strengths, the RPA strengths are shifted to higher energy region due to the repulsive particle-hole residual interaction generated mainly by exchanging $\rho$ meson. In addition to the characteristic peak of the IVGDR around energy of $20\mathrm{MeV}$ , low-lying dipole strengths appear at the excitation energy below $10\mathrm{MeV}$ . It is shown in Fig.1 that the effect of pairing correlations on the isovector dipole strength in $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ shifts the low-lying dipole strength to higher energy region and decreases the low-lying dipole strength, especially in $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ . The decrease of the low-lying strength is mainly due to the fact that two-quasiparticle excitations in the dipole mode are weakened by a factor of $\nu_{\alpha}^2$ when the pairing correlation is switched on. In addition, the two-quasiparticle excitation energy is larger than the corresponding particle-hole excitation energy in the dipole mode. On the contrary, when the pairing correlation is taken into account the configuration space becomes larger, which allows for the particle-particle and hole-hole transitions. This enlarged configuration space may increase the low-lying strength.

![](images/bb27207d862768da29b0573227e97201c9ac5970877f74579cace0617bcb4f64.jpg)

Fig. 1 Isovector dipole strength functions in neutron rich nuclei $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ (left panel) and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ (right panel)   
![](images/02244aab16e79092fd0b85ed08b53330500c9b773247c393154c930afa6a11a9.jpg)  
The QRRPA responses with the pairing (solid curves) are compared with the RRPA calculation without the pairing (short-dashed curves); the thick and thin curves represent perturbed and Hartree strengths, respectively

In comparison with the Hartree strength it is found that the RPA strength of the isovector dipole mode at the low energy region in $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ shown in Fig.1 remains at its position and is slightly attracted back to the lower energy. This is due to the correlations of the isoscalar operator in the isovector mode[12]. Differing from the normal IVGDR response, the low-lying resonance can be interpreted as the excitation of the excess neutrons out of phase with the core formed with an equal number of protons and neutrons[13]. Analyzing the Hartree strength of the isovector dipole mode in $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ at the energy below 10 MeV calculated with pairing correlations, one finds a pronounced peak around 8.5 MeV, which is formed mainly from the neutron configurations of $\nu (2s_{1/2}^{-1}2p_{3/2})$ (8.482 MeV) and $\nu (2s_{1/2}^{-1}2p_{1/2})$ (9.232 MeV), where the value in the parenthesis is its Hartree energy. Since 16 neutrons in $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ fill neutron orbits up to $2s_{1/2}$ and form a sub-closed shell, the occupation probabilities at $1d_{3/2}$ and $1f_{7/2}$ states in $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ are relatively small. Therefore the contribution from neutron states $1d_{3/2}$ and $1f_{7/2}$ to the low-lying Hartree strength is

insignificant. In contrast the Hartree strength at the low energy region in $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ , in addition to the peaks formed from the neutron configurations of $\nu (2s_{1/2}^{-1}2p_{3/2})$ (8.523 MeV) and $\nu (2s_{1/2}^{-1}2p_{1/2})$ (9.080 MeV), a few more peaks appear, which are formed from the neutron excitation between the bound level $1d_{3/2}$ and levels in the continuum. It is found that the Hartree strengths at the low-lying dipole in $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ are mainly due to neutron excitations near the Fermi surface.

In order to give a more clear description of those isovector low-lying dipole states obtained in the QRRPA approach, we calculate various moments of isovector dipole strengths at a given energy interval $E1$ and $E2$ : $m_{k} = \int_{E_{1}}^{E_{2}} R^{L}(E) E^{k} \mathrm{d}E$ , The RPA equation is solved till $E = 60 \mathrm{MeV}$ in the present calculations.

We calculate the moments in two energy intervals: $(0\mathrm{MeV}\leqslant E_x\leqslant 10\mathrm{MeV})$ and $(10\mathrm{MeV}\leqslant E_x\leqslant 30$ MeV), which are listed in table 1. In our present QRRPA calculations the low-lying isovector dipole strengths in $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ exhaust about $4.93\%$ and $5.85\%$ of the Thomas-Reiche-Kuhn (TRK) dipole sum rule, respectively. This results are in good agreement with the experimental analysis[12]. On the other hand, the cluster sum rule[14] is usually used to understand the properties of low-lying dipole strength in exotic nucleus excitations. Here we choose $^{20}\mathrm{Ne}$ as the core. In the table we also list the values obtained from TRK cluster dipole sum rule, say $17.2~\mathrm{e}^2\mathrm{fm}^2\mathrm{MeV}$ and $21.3~\mathrm{e}^2\mathrm{fm}^2\mathrm{MeV}$ for $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ , respectively. It shows that the low-lying dipole excitations in these two neutron rich nuclei exhaust about $26.3\%$ of the TRK cluster dipole sum rule.

Table 1 The moments and centroid energy of isovector dipole strengths in $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ in the QRRPA calculations   

<table><tr><td rowspan="2">Nuclei</td><td colspan="2">0 MeV≤Ex≤10 MeV</td><td colspan="2">10 MeV≤Ex≤30 MeV</td><td rowspan="2">STRK</td><td rowspan="2">SClus</td><td rowspan="2">E(0-10)</td><td rowspan="2">E(10-60)</td></tr><tr><td>m0</td><td>m1</td><td>m0</td><td>m1</td></tr><tr><td>26Ne</td><td>0.542</td><td>4.525</td><td>5.032</td><td>103.9</td><td>91.7</td><td>17.2</td><td>8.34</td><td>22.32</td></tr><tr><td>28Ne</td><td>0.705</td><td>5.606</td><td>5.648</td><td>111.2</td><td>95.8</td><td>21.3</td><td>7.94</td><td>21.13</td></tr></table>

Notes: The values in the sixth and seventh columns are obtained from the classical TRK dipole sum rule and TRK cluster sum rule $(\mathrm{e}^2\mathrm{fm}^2\mathrm{MeV})$ ; Values in the last two columns are the centroid energies in the two intervals; The units are $\mathrm{e}^2\mathrm{fm}^2\mathrm{MeV}$ and $\mathrm{e}^2\mathrm{fm}^2\mathrm{MeV}$ for $m_0$ and $m_{1}$ , respectively; All energy values are in unit of MeV

The centroid energy of the response function is defined as: $E = m_{1} / m_{0}$ . We separate the energy interval into two regions: 0 MeV $< E_{x} < 10$ MeV and 10 MeV $< E_{x} < 60$ MeV. The centroid energies in these two energy regions are listed in table 1. In the QRRPA calculations the centroid energies of low-lying isovector dipole strengths in $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ are 8.34 MeV and 7.94 MeV, respectively. Where as the centroid energies of the normal IVGDR strengths are located at 22.3 MeV for $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and 21.1 MeV for $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ .

In summary, we have studied the properties of low-lying isovector dipole resonances in neutron rich nuclei $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ in the framework of the QRRPA with the effective Lagrangian parameter set NL3. The ground state properties are calculated in the extended $\mathrm{RMF} + \mathrm{BCS}$ approach, where the resonant

continuum is properly treated. Constant pairing gaps extracted from the experimental binding energies of neighboring nuclei are adopted in the BCS calculation. In the QRRPA calculation the negative energy states in the Dirac sea are included due to the completeness. It is shown that the inclusion of pairing correlations has a relatively strong effect on the low-lying isovector dipole strength in neutron rich nuclei. In the QRRPA calculation the low-lying isovector dipole strengths in $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ exhaust about 5% of the Thomas-Reiche-Kuhn dipole sum rule. The centroid energies of the low-lying dipole excitation in nuclei $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ and $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ are located at the energy around 8.0 MeV. The theoretical prediction well accorded with the latest experimental analysis[2].

用准粒子的相对论无规位相近似研究了丰中子核 $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ 和 $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ 的低能同位旋矢量偶极激发强度。核基态性质由相对论平均场和推广的BCS对关联方法来得到，其中连续态中的单粒子能级用核的单粒子共振态来表示。研究结果表明丰中子核 $^{26}\mathrm{Ne}$ 和 $^{28}\mathrm{Ne}$ 在能量 $E_{x}<10 \mathrm{MeV}$ 的低能同位旋矢量偶极强度分别占Thomas-Reiche-Kuhn偶极求和规则的 $4.9\%$ 和 $5.8\%$ ，激发能的中心位置分别为 $8.3 \mathrm{MeV}$ 和 $7.9 \mathrm{MeV}$ 。理论预言与最新实验很好地符合。

# 6 Isospin-Dependent Optical Potentials in Dirac Brueckner-Hartree-Fock Approach*

Rong Jian $^{1}$ , Ma Zhongyu $^{1}$ , Nguyen van Giai $^{2}$

(1. China Institute of Atomic Energy, Beijing 102413, China;

2. Institut de Physique Nucl Éaire, IN2P3-CNRS, F-91406 Orsay Cedex, France)

The optical model potential is an essential tool in nuclear reaction studies. In the past 30 years there has been a great interest in the relativistic description of nuclear problems, due to the success in reproducing the spin rotation functions of proton elastic scattering[1] in the relativistic framework. In the relativistic approach, the spin-orbit coupling is directly related to the central (scalar and vector) potentials while in the non-relativistic approach it is usually adjusted independently. It is well known that the relativistic impulse approximation works successfully in the medium and high energy regions[2]. At low energies the medium effects become important, therefore the relativistic optical potential for the nucleon-nucleus elastic scattering is usually adopted. By fitting the experimental results, Clark et al.[3,4] got a reasonable global Dirac optical potential with a set of phenomenological parameters for proton-nucleus elastic scattering. It has given reasonable results for elastic scattering off stable nuclei. However, the predictive power of the phenomenological optical potentials is not guaranteed and therefore, extending them to reactions without experimental data, especially to those reactions involving unstable nuclei, remains questionable. It is necessary to investigate the optical potential microscopically in a more fundamental theory. Studies of the relativistic microscopic optical potential (RMOP) in the past years were based on several models, such as the $\mathrm{RMF}^{[5,6]}$ , relativistic Hartree-Fock (RHF)[7], and Dirac Brueckner-Hartree-Fock (DBHF)[8-10] approaches. The nucleon self-energies were calculated in symmetric nuclear matter and the isospin dependence was usually neglected. A reasonable description of the differential cross sections, analyzing powers and spin rotation functions of proton scattering off stable nuclei has been achieved in an energy range of 50-500 MeV without readjusting parameters[7,9,10].

With the development of experiments on newly constructed radioactive beam facilities, a series of new physical phenomena, such as the neutron and proton halo, have been observed in the past 20 years[11]. The density distributions of protons and neutrons in halo and exotic nuclei are very different from normal nuclei. Therefore, the isospin dependence of the RMOP becomes important. However, up to now the knowledge about the isospin dependence of the RMOP from experiments is very scarce.

In this work we investigate the isospin dependence of the RMOP in the framework of the DBHF approach. A new decomposition of the Dirac structure of nucleon self-energies, proposed recently by Schiller and Muether[12,13] is adopted. The DBHF $G$ -matrix is separated into a bare nucleon-nucleon (NN) interaction $V$ and a correlation term $\Delta G$ . A one boson exchange potential (OBEP) is employed for the bare NN interaction. A projection method is applied to the correlation term $\Delta G$ , which is parametrized by four pseudo-mesons. Therefore, the effective NN interaction $G$ in symmetric and asymmetric nuclear matter

can be characterized in the RHF approach by the exchanges of those four pseudo-mesons, in addition to the OBEP.

The optical potential of a nucleon in the nuclear medium is identified with the nucleon self-energy. The real part of the RMOP is evaluated in the DBHF approximation by adopting the decomposition of $G = V + \Delta G$ . In asymmetric nuclear matter the Fermi momenta of protons and neutrons are different. Therefore, proton and neutrons have to be distinguished. Here, we adopt simplified notations where proton and neutron indices are omitted unless when it is necessary. The nucleon self-energy is expressed as,

$$
\sum \left(k, k _ {\mathrm {F}}, \beta\right) = \sum_ {\alpha} \int \frac {\mathrm {d} ^ {4} q}{(2 \pi) ^ {4}} \left\{\Gamma_ {\alpha} ^ {a} \Delta_ {\alpha} ^ {a b} (0) T r \left[ i \Gamma_ {\alpha} ^ {b} G (q) \right] - i \Gamma_ {\alpha} ^ {a} \Delta_ {\alpha} ^ {a b} (q) \Gamma_ {\alpha} ^ {b} G (k - q) \right\}, \tag {1}
$$

where the first and second terms correspond to direct (Hartree) and exchange (Fock) terms, respectively. The index $\alpha$ refers to mesons, $a$ and $b$ are isospin components. $G$ is the single particle Green's function, and the $\Delta_{\alpha}^{ab}$ are meson propagators. The vertices $\Gamma_{\alpha}^{a}$ correspond to $i_g$ for scalar mesons, $-g\gamma^{\mu}$ for vector mesons and $-\gamma^{5}\gamma^{y}q_{u}f / m$ for pseudovector mesons. They are multiplied by the isospin operator $\iota_{a}$ for isovector mesons.

We extend this approach to investigate the imaginary part of the $G$ -matrix which is calculated as the $G$ -matrix polarization diagram. The contribution of the $G$ -matrix polarization diagram to the nucleon self-energy can be calculated as follows,

$$
\sum_ {\mathrm {p o l}} (k) = \sum_ {\alpha \beta} \int \frac {\mathrm {d} ^ {4} q}{(2 \pi) ^ {4}} \left\{\Gamma_ {\alpha} ^ {a} \Delta_ {\alpha} ^ {a b} (q) \int \frac {\mathrm {d} ^ {4} p}{(2 \pi) ^ {4}} T r \left[ \Gamma_ {\alpha} ^ {b} (i G (p)) \Gamma_ {\beta} ^ {c} (i G (p + q)) \right] \Delta_ {\beta} ^ {c d} (q) (i G (k - q)) \Gamma_ {\beta} ^ {d} \right\} \tag {2}
$$

To avoid ambiguity at low densities, and to simplify the calculation of the imaginary part of the RMOP, we adopt an effective phenomenological $G$ -matrix which contains four mesons with density-dependent coupling constants. The effective nucleon interaction consists of four scalar and vector pseudomesons with isoscalar and isovector characters, corresponding to $\sigma$ , $\omega$ , $\delta$ and $\rho$ mesons. The masses of these effective mesons are fixed and the coupling constants are adjusted in the RHF to reproduce the DBHF results in the scheme of $G = V + \Delta G$ , namely the neutron and proton self-energies as well as the binding energy at each density in the symmetric and asymmetric nuclear matter. The coupling constants are density dependent and depicted by the following expressions[14,15],

$$
g _ {i} (\rho) = g _ {i} \left(\rho_ {0}\right) f _ {i} (x)
$$

$$
f _ {i} (x) = a _ {i} \frac {1 + b _ {i} \left(x + d _ {i}\right) ^ {2}}{1 + c _ {i} \left(x + d _ {i}\right) ^ {2}} \tag {3}
$$

where $x = \rho / \rho_0$ , and $i = (\sigma, \omega, \delta, \rho)$ , $\rho$ and $\rho_0$ stand for the matter density and the saturation density of nuclear matter, respectively. $a_i, b_i, c_i, d_i$ are parameters determined by the fitting procedure.

The optical potential in finite nuclei is obtained by means of the local density approximation, where the space distribution of the RMOP is directly connected with the density and asymmetry parameter of the nuclear self-energies in the asymmetric nuclear matter.

$$
\Sigma_ {\mathrm {L D A}} (r, E) = \Sigma_ {\mathrm {N M}} (k, \rho (r), \beta) \tag {4}
$$

where $\Sigma_{\mathrm{LDA}}$ is the optical potential for a finite nucleus with the incident nucleon energy $E$ , and $\Sigma_{\mathrm{NM}}$ is the optical potential in nuclear matter at density $\rho$ and asymmetry parameter $\beta$ . The Dirac equation of a

nucleon with the incident energy $E$ in the nuclear medium has the form

$$
\left[ \vec {\alpha} \cdot \vec {p} + \gamma_ {0} \left(M + U _ {\mathrm {s}} ^ {\mathrm {t}}\right) + U _ {0} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \right] \psi^ {\mathrm {t}} (\vec {r}) = \varepsilon \psi^ {\mathrm {t}} (\vec {r}) \tag {5}
$$

where $U_{s} = \frac{\sum_{s} - \sum_{v}M}{1 + \sum_{v}}$ , $U_{0} = \frac{-\sum_{0} + E\sum_{v}}{1 + \sum_{v}}$ with $\varepsilon = E + M$ , $U_{s}^{t}$ and $U_{0}^{t}$ being the scalar and vector potential, respectively.

By eliminating the lower component of the Dirac spinor in Eq.5, a Schroedinger-type equation is obtained for the upper component:

$$
\left(- \frac {\nabla^ {2}}{2 E} + V _ {\mathrm {e f f}} ^ {\mathrm {t}} (r) + V _ {\mathrm {S . O .}} ^ {\mathrm {t}} (r) \sigma \cdot L + V _ {\text {D a r w i n}} ^ {\mathrm {t}} (r)\right) \varphi (r) = \frac {\varepsilon^ {2} - M ^ {2}}{2 E} \varphi (r) \tag {6}
$$

where $V_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{t}}, V_{\mathrm{S.O.}}^{\mathrm{t}}$ and $V_{\mathrm{Darwin}}^{\mathrm{t}}$ are the central, spin-orbit and Darwin potentials, respectively.

To analyze the proton-nucleus scattering reactions, the density distributions of finite nuclei should be first determined. In this work, the density distribution of finite nuclei are calculated in the relativistic mean field theory with the parameter set $\mathrm{NL3}^{[16]}$ since it describes well finite nuclei properties. We investigate elastic proton scattering off spherical nuclei, such as $\mathsf{p}^{+40}\mathrm{Ca}$ and $\mathsf{p}^{+208}\mathrm{Pb}$ . The density distribution of finite nuclei are calculated in the relativistic mean field theory with the parameter set $\mathrm{NL3}^{[16]}$ since it describes well finite nuclei properties. With the microscopic optical potential, we calculate the differential cross sections, analyzing powers and spin rotation functions of $\mathsf{p}^{+40}\mathrm{Ca}$ and $\mathsf{p}^{+208}\mathrm{Pb}$ at various values of $E_{\mathrm{p}} < 200\mathrm{MeV}$ and compare with the experimental data.

It is found that the calculated cross sections as well as spin observables are in rather good agreement with the experimental data at low energies. As the energy increases, the theoretical differential cross sections are overestimated at large angles in comparison with the experimental data, but they keep the same diffraction patterns. It seems that the Schroedinger equivalent potentials obtained in our calculations are a little too strong. It should be stressed that the RMOP is calculated microscopically with the nucleon effective interaction without readjusting parameters to fit the scattering data. The nucleon effective interaction is directly extracted from the DBHF $G$ -matrix. Since our calculations are parameter free in the scattering analysis, the quality of agreement with the experimental data is remarkable.

# 中文摘要

在Dirac Brueckner-Hartree-Fock(DBHF)理论下，研究了核子的相对论微观光学模型势(RMOP)，推广DBHF核子自能的Dirac结构的新的分解方法用于非对称核物质的研究。引进核子的有效相互作用来再现DBHF $G$ 矩阵计算的结果。在非对称核物质中核子自能的实部是用 $G$ 在Hartree-Fock近似下计算得到，而核子自能的虚部是由极化图来计算。有限核的光学势可以用定域密度近似由非对称核物质中核子的自能得到。从DBHF得到的核子-核散射的微观光学模型势，没有自由参数，计算的核子-核弹性散射，如 $\mathrm{p + }^{40}\mathrm{Ca},\mathrm{p + }^{208}\mathrm{Pb}$ 能够很好地符合实验结果。

# 7 Applications of Skyrme Energy-Density Functional to Fusion Reactions for Synthesis of Superheavy Nuclei*

Wang Ning, Wu Xizhen, Li Zhuxia, Liu Min

(China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-18, Beijing 102413, China)

It is of great importance to predict fusion cross sections and to analyze reaction mechanism for massive heavy-ion fusion reactions, especially for fusion reactions leading to superheavy nuclei. In those reactions, the calculation of the capture cross section is of crucial importance. In our previous paper, we applied the Skyrme energy density functional for the first time to study heavy ion fusion reactions. The barrier for fusion reaction was calculated by the Skyrme energy density functional together with the semi-classical extended Thomas-Fermi method.

Based on the fusion barrier obtained, we proposed a parametrization of the empirical barrier distribution to take into account the multi-dimensional character of the real barrier and then applied it to

calculate the fusion excitation functions of light and intermediate- heavy fusion reaction systems in terms of the barrier penetration concept. A large number of measured fusion excitation functions for energies around the fusion barriers were reproduced well. Now, the Skyrme energy-density functional approach has been extended to study fusion reactions leading to synthesis of superheavy nuclei. With the proton and neutron density distributions obtained by using a restricted density variational method with the Skyrme energy density functional together with the semi-classical extended Thomas-Fermi approach, the capture barriers of a series of massive fusion reactions also applicable for the synthesis of super-heavy nuclei were calculated by the same Skyrme energy density functional.

Based on the capture barriers obtained and the parameterized barrier distributions proposed, fusion and capture excitation functions of a large number of heavy-ion fusion reactions have been studied systematically. The average deviations of fusion cross sections of $92\%$ systems in 76 fusion reactions with $Z_{1}Z_{2} < 1$ 200 from experimental data are less than 0.05 at energies near and above the barriers. Massive fusion reactions, for example, the ${}^{238}\mathrm{U}$ -induced reactions and the ${}^{48}\mathrm{Ca} + {}^{208}\mathrm{Pb}$ have been studied and their capture excitation functions have been reproduced remarkably well. The influence of structure effects in the reaction partners on the capture cross sections are studied with the $\gamma$ value of the model. For a qualitative understanding of the enhancement ( $\gamma < 1$ ) and suppression ( $\gamma > 1$ ) of capture cross sections at sub-barrier fusion energies, the driving potential of the di-nuclear system model has been employed, in which a peak or valley appears respectively for the structure effects with $\gamma < 1$ and $\gamma > 1$ .

To search the most favorable condition for the synthesis of super-heavy nuclei, an optimal balance between the capture cross section and the excitation energy of the formed compound nuclei is studied by comparing the fusion reactions induced by the double magic nucleus $^{48}\mathrm{Ca}$ and by $^{32}\mathrm{S}$ and $^{35}\mathrm{Cl}$ . Based on this study, the "threshold" behavior of incident beam energy has been explored and possible values of this "threshold" for reactions mainly induced by $^{48}\mathrm{Ca}$ are given. Finally, we considered capture reactions leading to fusion with $^{36}\mathrm{S}$ , $^{37}\mathrm{Cl}$ , $^{48}\mathrm{Ca}$ and $^{50}\mathrm{Ti}$ bombarding on $^{248}\mathrm{Cm}$ , $^{247,249}\mathrm{Bk}$ , $^{250,252,254}\mathrm{Cf}$ and $^{252,254}\mathrm{Es}$ , and as well as the reactions $^{64}\mathrm{Ni} + ^{238}\mathrm{U}$ , $^{58}\mathrm{Fe} + ^{244}\mathrm{Pu}$ , $^{54}\mathrm{Cr} + ^{248}\mathrm{Cm}$ and $^{50}\mathrm{Ti} + ^{252}\mathrm{Cf}$ which lead to the same compound nucleus with $Z = 120$ and $N = 182$ . Data for these reactions have been provided from which we obtain very helpful information for the study of yet unmeasured massive fusion reactions. Especially, we predicted optimal fusion configuration and suitable incident beam energies for the synthesis of super-heavy nuclei.

# 中文摘要

应用Skyrme能量密度泛函方法计算融合反应位垒，通过对该位垒进行位垒分布的参数化处理，考虑进真实位垒的多维特性，在此基础上进行位垒穿透计算，得到俘获（融合）截面，在符合大量已有实验数据的基础上，通过寻找俘获截面和生成的超重复合核激发能的最佳平衡点，获取合成超重核的最佳反应条件，即最佳弹靶组合和最佳入射能。

# 8 Applications of Skyrme Energy-Density Functional to Fusion Reactions Spanning the Fusion Barriers

Liu Min, Wang Ning, Li Zhuxia, Wu Xizhen

(China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-18, Beijing 102413, China)

A large number of fusion excitation functions have been accumulated in recent decades, which provides a possibility for a systematic study on the fusion reactions. Newton analyzed a total of 46 fusion excitation functions at energies above the average fusion barriers using the Woods-Saxon form for the nuclear potential in a barrier passing model of fusion. They found that the empirical diffuseness parameter $a$ ranging between 0.75 and 1.5 were considerably larger than those obtained from elastic scattering data and the deduced $a$ showed strong increase with increasing the charge product $Z_{1}Z_{2}$ . Thus, it results in a certain difficulty for giving satisfied predictions of fusion cross sections for unmeasured reaction systems. The fusion coupled channel model is successful for describing fusion excitation function of heavy-ion reaction at energies near fusion barrier.

However, with the increasing of the neutron excess number and the charges (the product $Z_{1}Z_{2}$ ) of two nuclei, the fusion coupled channel model encounters a lot of difficulties due to a very large number of degrees of freedom involved. In order to carry out a systematic study of fusion excitation functions, a simple and useful approach seems to be required. Inspired by the success of the multi-dimensional barrier penetration method in describing the fusion excitation function, a reasonable parametrization of the weighing function of the multi-dimensional barrier based on the interaction potential in the entrance-channel seems to be very useful. According to the Hohenberg and Kohn theorem, the energy of an $N$ -body system of interaction fermions is a unique functional of local density. In the framework of the semiclassical Extended Thomas Fermi (ETF) approach together with a Skyrme effective nuclear interaction such a functional can be derived systematically. The density functional theory is widely used in the study of the nuclear ground state and the equilibrium nuclear systems at finite temperature which provides us with a useful balance between accuracy and computation cost allowing large systems with a simple self-consistent manner. Thus, it is very suitable to perform the calculation of the entrance-channel potential and the densities of reaction partners based on the same Skyrme energy density functional. This self-consistent treatment for both density distribution and the entrance-channel potential seems to be more reasonable. In this work, the Skyrme energy density functional is applied to make a systematic study of fusion reactions. Firstly, we use the semi-classical expressions of the Skyrme energy density functional to study the energies and the density distributions of a series of nuclei by the restricted density variational (RDV) method. Secondly, with the density distributions obtained, the entrance-channel potentials of a series of fusion reactions are calculated.

Then, based on the entrance-channel potential obtained, a parametrization of the multi-dimensional barrier is proposed by empirically considering the coupling to internal degrees of freedom such as the deformation and vibration motion of binary systems. The fusion excitation function for the multi-

dimensional barrier penetration is given by the integral with respect to one-dimensional barrier penetration and the weighing function of the barrier distribution. With this method, the fusion excitation functions for about 30 reaction systems are firstly investigated. In these reaction systems, the projectile and target nuclei are non-closed-shell but near the $\beta$ -stability line, therefore the shell effect and excess neutron effect are weak. From the calculations we find that for all reactions the fusion excitation functions can be reproduced very well. For studying fusion Reactions between nuclei with neutron-shell-closure or of neutron-rich nuclei, the structure effect factor in the weighing function correctly reflects the shell effect and the excess neutron effect, we also obtain very reasonable results.

For radioactive beam fusion reactions $^{132}\mathrm{Sn} + ^{64}\mathrm{Ni}$ , the competition also exists since $^{132}\mathrm{Sn}$ is both neutron-rich and neutron-shell-closure ( $N = 82$ , $Z = 50$ ). The structure factor value for this reaction is smaller than 1 and the enhancement of the fusion cross sections at sub-barrier energies is expected. Through compared with the experimental data and results of fusion coupled-channel model one finds that the sub-barrier fusion cross sections for this reaction is better described by our approach than the fusion coupled channel model calculations.

# 中文摘要

通过 Skyrme 能量密度泛函和约束密度变分方法得到一系列基态核的能量和密度分布，计算出融合反应位垒。通过位垒分布参数化方法考虑真实位垒的多维特性和闭壳效应以及过剩中子效应，对有实验数据的很多融合反应进行了系统研究，计算结果能很好地再现实验融合截面数据，并预言了一些融合反应的激发函数。

# 9 Investigation in Possibility of Producing Superheavy Fragments Through Massive Nuclear Reactions at Low Energies*

Wu Xizhen, Li Zhuxia, Wang Ning, Tian Junlong,

Zhao Kai, Zhang Yinxun, Ou Li, Liu Min

(China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-18, Beijing 102413)

Since the 70's the elements from $Z = 107$ to 116 were synthesized in the "cold fusion" reactions with lead and bismuth targets and "hot fusion" reactions with actinide targets. However, the further experimental extension of the region of SHE to the real "island" with the complete fusion reaction is limited by the number of available projectiles and targets, and by the very low production cross section. In order to explore new more neutron-rich superheavy regions the radioactive ion beams will have to be utilized, but up to now the intensive radioactive ion beams are not available. In this case an alternative pathway to the superheavy elements, so called the strongly damped collision process between massive has been studied. The evident advantage of this kind of reactions would be the very large mass transfer probability and the high neutron-to-proton ratio which makes it possible to reach neutron-rich areas in the "island of stability"

inaccessible to transfer or fusion reactions with lighter projectiles. Two main theoretical models, which are the quantum fluctuations within the fragmentation theory developed at 80 years and improved quantum molecular dynamics model developed recently by our group, are briefly described. In fragmentation theory, the set of nuclear shapes and collective coordinates (the separation distance, mass asymmetry $\eta$ , neck parameter and the quadrupole deformations of the fragments) are employed. A collective quantum-mechanical operator

$$
H = - \frac {\hbar^ {2}}{2} \sum_ {\lambda , \mu} \frac {1}{\sqrt {B}} \frac {\partial}{\partial \alpha_ {\lambda}} \sqrt {B} \left(B ^ {- 1}\right) _ {\alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {\mu}} \frac {\partial}{\partial \alpha_ {\mu}} + V (\alpha)
$$

is introduced and corresponding stationary equation is solved. The probability of fragmentation for a certain mass asymmetry is then obtained. In the microscopic transport model we study reactions of $^{244}\mathrm{Pu} + ^{244}\mathrm{Pu}$ , $^{238}\mathrm{U} + ^{238}\mathrm{U}$ and $^{197}\mathrm{Au} + ^{197}\mathrm{Au}$ . We explore the dynamic process of the strong damped reaction and find that the production probability of superheavy products with $Z > 114$ in the $^{244}\mathrm{Pu} + ^{244}\mathrm{Pu}$ reaction is much higher than that in the $^{238}\mathrm{U} + ^{238}\mathrm{U}$ reaction, and for the $^{197}\mathrm{Au} + ^{197}\mathrm{Au}$ reaction no products with $Z > 114$ has been found in the present study. The peak in the energy dependent production probability shows that the suitable selection of incident energy configurations in superheavy nuclei is very important for enhancing the probability of superheavy nuclei. The detail information about instantaneous structure and shape of superheavy products during the dynamic process has been obtained, which indicates that the exotic forms, for example, large deformed shape (band-like shape) could be one of stale configurations.

# 中文摘要

讨论了通过重核碰撞形成的复合体系破裂产生超重核的问题。评述两种主要的理论模型：即早期发展的在碎化理论框架下的量子涨落理论和本课题组尝试发展的微观输运理论模型。概括阐述了微观输运理论研究反应 $^{197}\mathrm{Au} + ^{197}\mathrm{Au}$ ， $^{238}\mathrm{U} + ^{238}\mathrm{U}$ 和 $^{244}\mathrm{Pu} + ^{244}\mathrm{Pu}$ 等的主要结果。这就是反应中产生的超重碎块的概率与入射能的关系，复合体系和超重碎块的衰变机制以及所形成的超重碎块的结合能和形状的分布。

# 10 Effects of Isospin Equilibrium on Cold Fusion of Superheavy Nuclei*

Liu Zuhua

(China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-18, Beijing 102413, China)

Synthesis of superheavy nuclei attracts much interest nowadays. In this work, we focus attention on the influence of the neutron flow, especially the isospin equilibrium in the cold fusion of superheavy nuclei. In the following, we take the reaction $^{58}\mathrm{Fe} + ^{208}\mathrm{Pb} \rightarrow {}^{165}\mathrm{Hs} + \mathrm{n}$ as an example to bring out the basic

physics of the possible effects of the isospin equilibrium on superheavy nucleus formation.

The formation cross section $(\sigma_{\mathrm{ER}})$ is taken to be the product of three factors: the cross section for the projectile and target to stick $(\sigma_{\mathrm{s}})$ , the probability for the sticking composite nucleus to reach the compound nucleus configuration $(1 - P_{\mathrm{qf}})$ , and the probability for the latter to survive fission $(P_{\mathrm{suvr}})$ . The second factor describes the competition between the compound nucleus formation and the quasi-fission. Here, $P_{\mathrm{qf}}$ is the probability of the quasi-fission. The sticking or capture cross sections were calculated using the formalisms of the neutron flow model[1] and the coupled-channels approach[2], respectively.

We assume that the dynamics in the second stage can be described by a diffusion process analogous to the motion of a swarm of Brownian particle suspend in a viscous fluid at temperature $T$ in the presence of repulsive parabolic potential $V_{\mathrm{af}}(x, y) = -a(y)(x - x_{\max}(y))^2 / 2$ . Here $x = s$ denotes the relative length between the effective surfaces of the approaching nuclei with $x_{\max}(y)$ locating the maximum in $V_{\mathrm{af}}$ , and $y = N$ represents the neutron numbers of the light nucleus. The probability distribution $W$ can be calculated with a two-parameter Smoluchowski equation:

$$
\frac {\partial W (x , y , t)}{\partial t} = \left[ L _ {x} (x, y) + \gamma L _ {y} (x, y) \right] W (x, y, t) \tag {1}
$$

where $L_{x}$ and $L_{y}$ are given by

$$
L _ {x} (x, y) = - \frac {\partial}{\partial x} D _ {x} (x, y) + D _ {x x} \frac {\partial^ {2}}{\partial x ^ {2}} \tag {2}
$$

$$
L _ {y} (x, y) = - \frac {\partial}{\partial y} D _ {y} (x, y) + D _ {y y} \frac {\partial^ {2}}{\partial y ^ {2}} \tag {3}
$$

We assume that the diffusion coefficients, $D_{xx}$ and $D_{yy}$ are constants. The drift coefficient $D_x$ contains the driving force, $D_x = a(y)(x - x_{\max}) / \alpha_x$ . The drift coefficient $D_y$ is proportional to the driving force in the $y$ -direction and can be calculated with the potential governing the neutron flow[3].

In Eq. (1) we introduced a parameter $\gamma$ to indicate the different time scales. It is well established that in low energy heavy-ion collision, the $N / Z$ equilibrium happens on a time scale much faster than other collective motions, such as the change in the overall length of the configuration. In the limit of $\gamma$ large more than 1, Eq. (1) reduced to a one-parameter Smoluchowski equation[3]:

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial t} W (x, t) = L _ {0, 0} W (x, t) \tag {4}
$$

The operator $L_{0,0}$ has the form

$$
L _ {0, 0} = - \frac {\partial}{\partial x} \bar {D} _ {x} (x) + D _ {x x} \frac {\partial^ {2}}{\partial x ^ {2}} \tag {5}
$$

with

$$
\bar {D} _ {x} (x) = \int D _ {x} (x, y) \phi_ {0} (y, x) d y \tag {6}
$$

Here, $\phi_0$ is the eigenfunction of the operator $L_{\mathbf{y}}$ for $n = 0$ :

$$
\phi_ {0} (y, x) = \sqrt {\frac {b (x)}{2 \pi k T}} \exp \left[ - \frac {b (x) \left(y - y _ {\mathrm {e q}}\right) ^ {2}}{2 k T} \right] \tag {7}
$$

The solution of the Fokker-Plank Eq. (4) turns out to be a Gaussian. The probability to reach the

compound nucleus configuration is equal to the area under the distribution's tail in the region of negative $x$ -values[4]

$$
1 - P _ {\mathrm {q f}} = \frac {1}{2} \operatorname {e r f c} \sqrt {\bar {\beta}} \tag {8}
$$

where $\overline{\beta} = \overline{B} / kT$ , and erfc is the error function complement, equal to (1—erf). The average barrier height reads

$$
\bar {B} = \int B (y) \phi_ {0} (y, x _ {0}) d y \tag {9}
$$

The probability to survive fission and to emit only one neutron $(P_{\mathrm{suvr}})$ is treated according to more or less conventional formulae[4]. Data of the neutron separation energy, fission barrier heights and shell correction are taken from references[5-7]. Fig.1 compares the predicted excitation functions for the reaction $^{58}\mathrm{Fe} + ^{208}\mathrm{Pb}\rightarrow{}^{165}\mathrm{Hs} + \mathrm{n}$ with experimental data[8].

The solid and dashed curves are the excitation functions of the theories with isospin equilibrium, while the dotted and dash-dotted lines represent the results of the ones without isospin equilibrium. The sticking cross sections for the solid and dash-dotted lines are calculated with the neutron flow model[1], for the dashed and dotted lines they are calculated with the coupled-channels model[2].

Our calculation of neutron flow model[1] shows that as far as at $s = 3$ fm neutrons can flow freely between the approaching nuclei $^{58}\mathrm{Fe}$ and $^{208}\mathrm{Pb}$ . As a result of neutron flow the $N / Z$ , i.e., the $z$ -direction projection of isospin rapidly reaches an equilibrium distribution, meanwhile the system, originally in the fusion valley, is injected into the asymmetric fission valley. The dynamic process of the composite nucleus in the asymmetric fission valley is treated with the Smoluchowski equation. It is shown that the probability to overcome the asymmetric fission barrier and achieve compound nucleus configuration, hence the fusion cross section is obviously suppressed due to the effect of isospin equilibrium.

Fig. 1 Comparison of the theoretical excitation functions   
![](images/7fa0167da262300697a07bac0256331fcdb89ee2652b64a3ba5ca6fa1fdf4abc.jpg)  
for the reaction $^{58}\mathrm{Fe} + ^{208}\mathrm{Pb} \rightarrow {}^{165}\mathrm{Hs} + \mathrm{n}$ with experimental data[8]
