利用二参量的Smoluchowski方程计算了 ${}^{58}\mathrm{Fe} + {}^{208}\mathrm{Pb} \rightarrow {}^{165}\mathrm{Hs} + \mathrm{n}$ 反应合成 ${}^{265}\mathrm{Hs}$ 的形成截面。计算中考虑了中子流动和中子-质子平衡，考察了中子流动对超重核合成的影响。

# 11 Application of the Self-Consistent Collective Coordinate Method to Multi-O(4) Model

Gu Jianzhong

(China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-18, Beijing 102413, China)

Microscopic description of large amplitude collective motions in nuclei is a long-standing fundamental subject of nuclear structure physics. The self-consistent collective coordinate (SCC) method which was developed by a Japanese group in $1980s^{[1]}$ is a promising theory for this subject. It deeply roots in the time-dependent Hartree-Fock theory and has a beautiful and compact mathematical formulation. The SCC method has been successfully applied to kinds of anharmonic vibrations and high spin rotations, mainly by Kenichi Matsuyanagi and his co-workers [2]. The successful application substantiated the SCC method and illuminated its beauty and power.

The SCC method allows us to derive a collective Hamiltonian which describes the collective motion of a system. The collective Hamiltonian predicts the energy spectrum and transition strength of collective motion of the system. Being observable, the predicted ones can be tested by experimental data. The O(4) model captures the main features of nuclear forces and is suited to simulate the competition between the pairing of nucleons and deformation of nuclei. Based on the SCC method, we have derived the collective Hamiltonians for the single-shell O(4) model and for the multi-shell O(4) model in the spherical case. In the deformed case, the collective Hamiltonian for the multi-shell O(4) model was also calculated, which serves a starting point to study nuclear shape coexistence phenomena. We developed computer codes to

calculate the spectra of the collective Hamiltonians and the quadrupole transition strengths among low-lying states. When the collective state vector evolves from a spherical point our results are consistent with those given by the exact solution of the multi-O(4) model.

We studied microscopically and self-consistently the nuclear shape coexistence phenomena by using the coupled-configuration SCC method suggested by Fukui[3]. The diabatic picture is used in this approach. Namely, one first, based on the SCC method, defines two diabatic configurations which correspond to the prolate and the oblate states, neglecting the couplings between them. The couplings between the two kinds of states are then treated in a manner similar to the well-known coupled channel method. We applied the coupled-configuration SCC method to the multi-shell O(4) model. We have calculated the couplings, low-lying states and transition strengths. They are in good agreement with the exact solutions, those given by the diabatic $\mathrm{SCC}^{[4]}$ and those given by the generator coordinate method. In future we will apply the coupled configuration SCC method to a model with pairing-plus-quadrupole interactions, and finally to a model with realistic interactions. We shall pay our particular attention to the dynamical origin of the shape coexistence. This research will clarify the mechanism of the shape coexistence, then enrich and deepen our understanding of the shape coexistence.

# References

[1] Marumori T, Maskawa T, Sakata F, et al. Prog Theor Phys, 1980, 64: 1294.   
[2] Matsuo M, Matsuyanagi K. Prog Theor Phys, 1985, 74: 1 227.   
[3] Takahiro Fukui, Masayuki Matsuo, Kenichi Matsuyanagi. Prog Theor Phys, 1991, 85: 281.   
[4] Kobayasi M, Nakatsukasa T, Matsuo M, Matsuyanagi K. Prog Theor Phys, 2003, 110: 65.

# 中文摘要

在时间依赖的 Hartree-Bogoliubov 理论框架下，基于自洽集体坐标方法解析导出了直至四阶的多壳 O(4) 模型的集体哈密顿量。在单壳及球对称两种特殊情况下，该哈密顿量的正确性得到了严格检验。该哈密顿量是研究原子核形状共存的一个很好的出发点。

# 1 The Astrophysical $^8\mathrm{Li}$ $(\mathbf{n},\gamma)$ $^9\mathrm{Li}$ Reaction Rate

Li Zhihong, Liu Weiping, Bai Xixiang, Guo Bing, Lian Gang, Yan Shengquan, Wang Baoxiang, Lu Yun, Zeng Sheng, Chen Yongshou, Wu Kaisu, Shu Nengchuan (China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-46, Beijing 102413, China)

The $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^9\mathrm{Li}$ reaction has attracted much attention in the recent years because of its importance in astrophysics. Nucleosynthesis of light nuclides is impeded by the gap at mass number $A = 8$ , where no stable nuclei exist. In the explosive neutron-rich environments, however, this gap can be bridged with reactions involving the unstable nucleus $^8\mathrm{Li}$ to synthesize the nuclides beyond $^7\mathrm{Li}$ . Type II supernovae and inhomogeneous big bang nucleosynthesis are thought to be the astrophysical sites for such nucleosynthetic processes.

In the recently developed scenario of the $r$ -process nucleosynthesis occurring in the circumstances of type II supernovae[1], ${}^{4}\mathrm{He}(\mathfrak{t},\gamma)^{7}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\alpha ,\mathfrak{n})^{11}\mathrm{B}$ becomes one of the active reaction chains to produce seed nuclei. While the ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ reaction, that leads to the production of ${}^{9}\mathrm{Be}$ via the ${}^{9}\mathrm{Li}-\beta^{-}$ decay, competes with the ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\alpha ,\mathfrak{n})^{11}\mathrm{B}$ reaction. The competition determines which reaction path is favored. This reaction also competes with the ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}-\beta^{-}$ decays. Depending on the rate, ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ may reduce the stellar production of $A>12$ nuclei to some extent. Recently, binary neutron star mergers[2] have been proposed as the alternative sites for the r-process, in which similar situation is also found.

As for big bang nucleosynthesis, the inhomogeneous models (IBBNs)[3] predict a higher production of $A > 8$ nuclides than the standard model (SBBN) does. The poor agreement[4] between the observed primordial abundances and predicted ones with the SBBN has indicated the need for the IBBNs. In the inhomogeneous models, the major reaction chain forming heavier nuclei begins with ${}^{7}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\alpha ,\mathfrak{n})^{11}\mathrm{B}$ , although it has been found[5] that ${}^{7}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^{9}\mathrm{Li}(\alpha ,\mathfrak{n})^{12}\mathrm{B}$ may play a more important role. Thus the ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ reaction affects not only the reaction path to $A > 8$ isotopes but also the abundances of Li, Be, B and C.

Considerable effort has been devoted to experimentally determining the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\alpha, \mathfrak{n})^{11}\mathrm{B}$ cross section, a large uncertainty still remains for the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n}, \gamma)^9\mathrm{Li}$ cross section. There were some microscopic and systematic calculations of this reaction that deviated by order of magnitude. Direct measurement of the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n}, \gamma)^9\mathrm{Li}$ reaction is impossible because no neutron target exists and the half life of $^8\mathrm{Li}$ is too short (838 ms) as a target. The only experimental information was obtained from two Coulomb dissociation measurements that presented the different upper limits. It is therefore highly needed to measure the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n}, \gamma)^9\mathrm{Li}$ cross section through an independent approach. It is a practicable method to extract the direct capture cross section for the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n}, \gamma)^9\mathrm{Li}$ reaction using the direct capture model and spectroscopic factor which can be deduced from the measurement of angular distribution for the transfer reaction $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{d}, \mathfrak{p})^9\mathrm{Li}$ .

We measured the angular distribution of $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{p})^9\mathrm{Li}$ reaction at $E_{\mathrm{c.m.}} = 7.8$ MeV and deduced the single particle spectroscopic factor of the ground state of $^9\mathrm{Li} = ^8\mathrm{Li} + \mathrm{n}$ , being $0.68 \pm 0.14$ , based on DWBA analysis. The $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^9\mathrm{Li}_{\mathrm{g.s.}}$ cross section was calculated by assuming that the reaction

proceeds by direct E1 capture of neutrons. At low energies of astrophysical interest, the capture reaction is almost totally determined by the $s$ -wave neutron capture process. The cross section for E1 capture of neutron to the ground state of $^9\mathrm{Li}$ with the orbital and total angular momenta $l_{f}$ and $j_{f}$ is given by

$$
\sigma (\mathrm {n}, \gamma) = \frac {1 6 \pi}{9} \left(\frac {E _ {\gamma}}{\hbar c}\right) ^ {3} \frac {e _ {\mathrm {e f f}} ^ {2}}{k ^ {2}} \frac {1}{\hbar \nu} \frac {2 j _ {f} + 1}{(2 I _ {1} + 1) (2 I _ {2} + 1)} S _ {l _ {f}, j _ {f}} | \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} r ^ {2} w _ {l _ {i}} (k r) u _ {l _ {f}} (r) \mathrm {d} r | ^ {2}
$$

where $E_{\gamma}$ stands for the $\gamma$ -ray energy, $\nu$ is the relative velocity between neutron and ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}$ , $k$ the incident wave number, $I_{i}$ is the spin of particle $i$ . The effective charge $e_{\mathrm{eff}} = -eZ / (A + 1)$ for neutron, $Z$ and $A$ are the proton and mass numbers of ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}$ . $w_{l_i}(kr)$ is the distorted radial wave function in the entrance channel, $u_{l_f}(r)$ is the single particle wave function of the ${}^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ ground state. Taking the present experimental spectroscopic factor $(S_{lj})$ value, the cross section for the direct capture reaction ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{n},\gamma)^{9}\mathrm{Li}_{\mathrm{s.s.}}$ was calculated as a function of incident energy. Fig.1 demonstrates the temperature dependence of the reaction rate for the direct capture in ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{n},\gamma)^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ together with that for the resonant capture at $5/2^{-}4.3 \mathrm{MeV}$ state in ${}^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ , as well as the total reaction rate. Direct capture to the first excited state at $2.69 \mathrm{MeV}$ is not included in the calculation since the transition strength is believed to be negligible. It can be clearly seen that the direct capture plays an important role in the ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{n},\gamma)^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ reaction especially in the astrophysical environment of $T_{9}<1$ . The reaction rate for the direct capture was found to be $(3970 \pm 950) \mathrm{cm}^{3} \cdot \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \cdot \mathrm{s}^{-1}$ at $T_{9}=1$ , the uncertainty arises from the errors of spectroscopic factor and the volume integral of potential per nucleon.

In summary angular distribution of the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{p})^{9}\mathrm{Li}_{\mathrm{g.s.}}$ reaction at $E_{\mathrm{c.m.}} = 7.8 \mathrm{MeV}$ was measured in inverse kinematics using coincidence detection of $^8\mathrm{Li}$ and recoil proton, for the first time. Based on DWBA analysis, the single particle spectroscopic factor, $S_{1,3/2}$ , for the ground state of $^9\mathrm{Li} = ^8\mathrm{Li} + \mathrm{n}$ was derived to be $0.68 \pm 0.14$ , and then used to calculate the direct capture cross sections for the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^9\mathrm{Li}_{\mathrm{g.s.}}$ reaction at energies of astrophysical interest. This presents first experimental constraints for the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^9\mathrm{Li}$ reaction rate that is of astrophysical interest.

![](images/cf0a42bd1ccbbb791158e231f1456f1507a2724b4c0a7d4e8169b1886de5997f.jpg)  
Fig. 1 Temperature dependence of the reaction rates for ${}^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathbf{n},\gamma)^9\mathrm{Li}$

# 中文摘要

${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ 反应在二类超新星及大爆炸核合成中具有重要意义，引起了众多核天体物理学家的兴趣。由于没有中子和短寿命放射性反应靶， ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ 反应的直接测量很难在可预计的时间内完成。在串列加速器次级束流线上，采取 ${}^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ 与质子符合的实验方案，给出了质心系 $E_{\mathrm{c.m.}} = 7.8$ MeV 的 ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{d},\mathfrak{p})^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ 反应角分布。通过DWBA计算拟合出 ${}^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ 基态的单粒子谱因子为 $0.68\pm 0.14$ 。根据中子的辐射俘获理论,进一步导出了 ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\gamma)^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ 反应的截面和天体物理反应率。

# 2 A Setup for Resonant Scattering Reactions With Thick Target

Wang Youbao, Wang Baoxiang, Bai Xixiang, Guo Bing, Li Zhihong, Lian Gang, Liu Weiping, Su Jun, Yan Shengquan, Zeng Sheng

(China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-46, Beijing 102413, China)

The thick-target method for resonant scattering reactions using secondary radioactive ion beams in inverse kinematics attracts a lot of attention, recently. The method makes use of a target, for example $(\mathrm{CH}_2)_n$ , which is thick enough to stop the secondary beams. The measured proton spectrum, after correction of reaction kinematics and energy loss, reveals the excitation function of elastic resonant reaction. A fit of the excitation function gives rise to the energy, spin-parity and $\Gamma_{\mathrm{p}}$ of the resonances. The advantage of the method is that the excitation function for a certain energy range can be obtained by a one-shot run, thus is suitable for the currently available low-intensity secondary beams.

For the thick-target experiments using the secondary beam line at HI-13 tandem laboratory, a setup based on the use of a time-of-flight (TOF) system and large-area double-sided silicon strip detector (DSSSD) is prepared. A schematic drawing of the setup is shown in Fig. 1.

The TOF system is applied for the identification of the components in secondary beams. A gate on the wanted components in the TOF spectrum can be used for the normalization of the cross section, and also for the elimination of the random $\beta^{+}$ background arisen from the decay of the stopped beams in $(\mathrm{CH}_2)_n$ target. For this purpose, two plastic scintillator timing detectors are used, each of which is

composed of a $10\mu \mathrm{m}$ thick BC422 scintillator foil coupled with a Hamamatsu R2083 photomultiplier tube (PMT). The flight path of TOF is $3590~\mathrm{mm}$ .

![](images/43ba560124e9d336f764939e466e4a3de149b32c88a8f7bb203752bcd417fcb8.jpg)  
Fig. 1 Thick-target experimental setup

To cover the full energy range of emitted protons, a $\Delta$ E-E telescope which consists of a $63\mu \mathrm{m}$ thick DSSSD backed with a $982\mu \mathrm{m}$ thick silicon detector is installed. Both detectors are of the size of $50\mathrm{mm} \times 50\mathrm{mm}$ , the DSSSD is position sensitive with a resolution of $3\mathrm{mm} \times 3\mathrm{mm}$ , defined by the width of microstrips orthogonally oriented on both sides. The $\Delta$ E-E telescope is placed at $150\mathrm{mm}$ downstream from the $(\mathrm{CH}_2)_n$ target to enhance the angular resolution; in addition, the beam spot is refined by a $5\mathrm{mm}$ and $3\mathrm{mm}$ in diameter collimator complex.

The setup is tested with a $^{17}\mathrm{F}$ secondary beam of about 60 MeV on a $(\mathrm{CH}_2)_n$ target of 7.53 $\mathrm{mg/cm^2}$ . As an example, a TOF spectrum is shown in Fig. 2. The purity of $^{17}\mathrm{F}$ is of 44% in this run, which is much less than the best ever achieved of 82%. The $^{17}\mathrm{F}$ can be identified in TOF with a resolution of 4.5 ns in FWHM.

![](images/97adc39614fd5de898deb56b2cdfd37d4eefb1d46b4f451918bd72854a8f92df.jpg)  
Fig.2 A TOF spectrum   
The hatched area corresponds to $^{17}\mathrm{F}$ produced via ${}^{2}\mathrm{H}(^{16}\mathrm{O},{}^{17}\mathrm{F})\mathrm{n}$ reaction

A Monte-Carlo simulation of the $\Delta$ E-E telescope is performed under the same condition. The proton total energy in the center of mass (c.m.) frame versus energy loss in DSSSD is given in Fig.3. It is shown that the threshold is about $200\mathrm{keV}$ and the resolution is of about $100\mathrm{keV}$ , both in c.m. frame. Further improvement of the energy resolution can be achieved by analyzing the TOF spectrum.

Experiment with the setup using $^{17}\mathrm{F}$ secondary beam for the $^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ excited states of nuclear astrophysical interest has been finished. Analysis of the experimental data is currently in process.

![](images/c10c14b2df20a39ff679fe7c6035ab3167ea5a8e2792505339a84a69d019eee6.jpg)  
Fig. 3 The proton total energy in the c.m. frame versus energy loss in DSSSD

# 中文摘要

次级束弹性共振散射反应的厚靶实验方法目前在国际上颇受重视。此方法使用较厚的固体反应靶，如 $(\mathrm{CH}_2)_n$ 等，使束流的能量全部损失在靶中，在反应靶的下游测量共振散射反应出射的质子；经反应运动学和能量损失修正，可一次得到较大能量范围内弹性共振反应的激发函数。通过对质子能谱的拟合，进一步导出共振态的能量、自旋宇称和发射质子的分宽度等共振量子数。此方法的优点是可用一个能量点的、流强相对较弱的放射性束，一次测量较大能量范围内共振散射反应的激发函数。为能在中国原子能科学研究院的次级束装置上开展弹性共振散射反应的厚靶实验，建立了1套包含飞行时间及大面积双面硅条探测器的探测系统。用蒙卡方法对 $\Delta \mathrm{E - E}$ 组合进行了模拟。用 $^{17}\mathrm{F}$ 次级束的弹性共振散射反应来研究核天体物理感兴趣的 $^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ 激发态的实验刚刚完成，数据处理正在进行中。

# 3 Measurement of $^{17}\mathbf{F}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{n})^{18}\mathbf{Ne}$ Reaction

Yan Shengquan, Lian Gang, Li Zhihong, Liu Weiping, Wang Youbao,

Bai Xixiang, Zeng Sheng, Guo Bing, Wang Baoxiang, Su Jun

(China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-36, Beijing 102413, China)

Explosive hydrogen burning occurs in very massive ( $M \geqslant 10^{5} - 10^{8} M_{\odot}$ ) star with high temperature and density. Hot pp chain, hot CNO, hot NeNa-MgAl chain and the flowing rp $\alpha$ p process will play a prominent role in hydrogen burning as the temperature goes higher. When the temperature of the star

is higher than $10^{8}\mathrm{K}$ , high temperature CNO chain is dominant in hydrogen burning, with the temperature goes higher to $5\times 10^{8}\mathrm{K}$ , CNO chain will transfer to $\mathrm{NeNa - MgAl}$ chain. ${}^{14}\mathrm{O}(\alpha ,\mathfrak{p}){}^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)$ ${}^{18}\mathrm{Ne}(\alpha ,\mathfrak{p})^{21}\mathrm{Na}$ and ${}^{15}\mathrm{O}(\alpha ,\gamma)^{19}\mathrm{Ne}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^{20}\mathrm{Na}$ reaction chains included in high temperature CNO chain and the high temperature CNO leak mechanism affected the nucleosynthesis path and energy generate rate greatly, ${}^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ is one of the important reactions which involve radioactive nuclei. The cross section of the ${}^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ reaction is very small at energy range of astrophysical interest. The angular distribution of ${}^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathfrak{d},\mathfrak{n})^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ was used to extract the asymptotic normalization constant (ANC) for the virtual decay of ${}^{18}\mathrm{Ne}\rightarrow{}^{17}\mathrm{F} + \mathrm{p}$ and the astrophysical $S$ -factor for direct capture contribution in ${}^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ .

The measurement of the $^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{n})^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ reaction was carried out in the secondary beam facility of the HI-13 tandem accelerator at China Institute of Atomic Energy, Beijing. A 97 MeV $^{16}\mathrm{O}$ primary beam from the tandem accelerator impinged on a $\mathrm{D}_2$ gas target and $^{17}\mathrm{F}$ ions were produced through the $^{16}\mathrm{O}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{n})^{17}\mathrm{F}$ reaction. After the magnetic separation and focus, the 71.8 MeV $^{17}\mathrm{F}$ secondary beam was delivered. The typical purity of $^{17}\mathrm{F}$ beam was about 80% and the energy resolution was about 10%.

The $^{17}\mathrm{F}$ beam was then collimated with two apertures in diameter of $3\mathrm{mm}$ and directed onto a $1.25\mathrm{mg/cm}^2$ thick $(\mathrm{CD}_2)_n$ target to study the $^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{n})^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ reaction. The reaction products $^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ were detected and identified with a $\Delta \mathrm{E}-\mathrm{E}$ telescope, setup was shown in Fig.1. The telescope consists of a $19.3\mu \mathrm{m}$ silicon $\Delta \mathrm{E}$ detector and a $300\mu \mathrm{m}$ 10-annular silicon E detector backed by an independent $300\mu \mathrm{m}$ silicon detector. Such a detector configuration covered the laboratory angular ranges of $0^\circ$ to $6.5^\circ$ (greater than the maximum $\theta_{\mathrm{lab}}$ of $^{17}\mathrm{F}$ , $6.2^\circ$ , thus enabled $100\%$ detection of $^{17}\mathrm{F}$ ions due to the inverse kinematics).

We have got $3.35 \times 10^{8} \mathrm{~F}$ events, and the angular distribution of $^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{n})^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ is shown in Fig.2. Because of the poor energy divergence of the secondary beam, the angular distribution for $\theta_{\mathrm{c.m.}} > 45^{\circ}$ is hard to extract. The theoretical analysis of the data is in progress.

![](images/1be02a1aefcdcf4dc0b00078db5b4dc3c3f855120368b83dc015f9f3c94ad05b.jpg)  
Fig.1 Experimental setup

![](images/cd69f634eb9c22c2adc1942006cd24831de7f512e5f8f402941c983feb4d71fc.jpg)  
Fig. 2 Angular distribution of the $^{17}\mathbf{F}(\mathbf{d}, \mathbf{n})^{18}\mathbf{Ne}$ reaction

# 中文摘要

${}^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ 是高温CNO链及其泄漏机制所包括的 ${}^{14}\mathrm{O}(\alpha ,\mathrm{p}){}^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^{18}\mathrm{Ne}(\alpha ,\mathrm{p})^{21}\mathrm{Na}$ 和 ${}^{15}\mathrm{O}(\alpha ,$ $\gamma)^{19}\mathrm{Ne}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^{20}\mathrm{Na}$ 中的一个重要反应。在HI-13串列加速器次级束流线上， $97\mathrm{MeV}$ 的 ${}^{16}\mathrm{O}$ 轰击次级束流线上的氘气靶，通过 ${}^{16}\mathrm{O}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{n})^{17}\mathrm{F}$ 反应得到能量为 $71.8\mathrm{MeV}$ 的 ${}^{17}\mathrm{F}$ 放射性束流(纯度 $85\%$ )。用 ${}^{17}\mathrm{F}$ 打 $\mathrm{CD}_2$ 靶，设置 $\Delta \mathrm{E - E}$ （环状）望远镜测量逆运动学反应 ${}^2\mathrm{H}({}^{17}\mathrm{F},{}^{18}\mathrm{Ne})\mathfrak{n}$ 的产物 ${}^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ 。从而得到转移反应 ${}^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{n})^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ 反应的角分布，并利用渐进归一化系数(ANC)确定 ${}^{17}\mathrm{F}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^{18}\mathrm{Ne}$ 反应的天体物理 $S$ 因子。目前数据的理论分析正在进行中。

# 4 Determination of Astrophysical Reaction Rate of ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{p})^{9}\mathrm{Li}$

Guo Bing, Li Zhihong, Liu Weiping, Bai Xixiang, Lian Gang, Han Yinlu,

Yan Shengquan, Wang Baoxiang, Zeng Sheng, Su Jun, Lu Yun

(China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-46, Beijing 102413, China)

The standard baryon homogeneous big-bang model (SBBN)[1,2] has successfully predicted the primordial nucleosynthesis of elements up to $^7\mathrm{Li}$ and synthesized very tiny quantities of the heavier nuclides, primarily because no stable nuclei exist at mass number $A = 8$ . In contrast to the SBBN, the baryon inhomogeneous big-bang models for primordial nucleosynthesis (IBBNs)[3-5] take density nonuniformity into account and involve many nuclear reactions of unstable nuclei which can bridge the stability gap at $A = 8$ , and predict a higher production of nuclides beyond $^7\mathrm{Li}$ . All the reactions for producing or destroying $^8\mathrm{Li}$ and $^8\mathrm{B}$ are of importance. With regard to $^8\mathrm{Li}$ , its generating reactions are $^7\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n}, \gamma)^8\mathrm{Li}$ and $^7\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{d}, \mathfrak{p})^8\mathrm{Li}$ ; destroying ones are $^8\mathrm{Li}(\alpha, \mathfrak{n})^{11}\mathrm{B}$ , $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n}, \gamma)^9\mathrm{Li}$ , $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{d}, \mathfrak{p})^9\mathrm{Li}$ , $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{d}, \mathfrak{t})^7\mathrm{Li}$ , $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{d}, \mathfrak{n})^9\mathrm{Be}$ , $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{p}, \alpha)^5\mathrm{He}$ and so on.

So far, the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{p})^9\mathrm{Li}$ reaction was only measured in 1990's [6], where an upper limit of the cross section was presented due to no $^9\mathrm{Li}$ events detected. In present work, we measured the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{p})^9\mathrm{Li}_{\mathrm{g.s.}}$ angular distribution with a 39 MeV secondary $^8\mathrm{Li}$ beam in inverse kinematics using the secondary beam facility GIRAFFE[7, 8] at the HI-13 tandem accelerator of China Institute of Atomic Energy. The cross section for the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{p})^9\mathrm{Li}_{\mathrm{g.s.}}$ reaction at $E_{\mathrm{c.m.}} = 7.8$ MeV was then determined to be (7.9±2.0) mb. The error was caused by statistical uncertainty and the difference of optical potentials used in DWBA calculation. Based on the measured cross section of the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{p})^9\mathrm{Li}_{\mathrm{g.s.}}$ reaction, its excitation

function was computed through the optical model, the unified Hauser-Feshbach and exciton model[9] and DWBA theory, as shown in Fig.1.

![](images/6fa757afd89d089fc029bb17bb588507c94aa827a0f446c07625d708d2cae002.jpg)  
Fig. 1 $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathbf{d}, \mathbf{p})^9\mathrm{Li}_{\mathrm{g.s}}$ cross sections for the present experiment and theoretical extrapolation

For two-body reaction, the reaction rate is computed as

$$
N (\sigma v) = N _ {\mathrm {A}} \frac {(8 / \pi) ^ {1 / 2}}{\mu^ {1 / 2} \left(k _ {\mathrm {B}} T\right) ^ {3 / 2}} \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} \sigma E \exp (- E / k _ {\mathrm {B}} T) \mathrm {d} E \tag {1}
$$

where $N_{\mathrm{A}}$ is the Avogadro number, $\mu$ the reduced mass, $k_{\mathrm{B}}$ the Boltzmann constant, $T$ the temperature, $E$ the energy in center-of-mass frame. The temperature dependence of reaction rate was derived from equation 1, for the first time, as shown in Fig.2.

![](images/6514feec5aebfa355e77d52f04ba993ff399a2f68bf3e653baf05bc2f785b5bf.jpg)  
Fig. 2 Temperature dependence of the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{p})^9\mathrm{Li}_{\mathrm{g.s.}}$ reaction rate

[4] Jedamzik K, Fuller G M, Mathews G J, et al. Astrophys J, 1994, 422: 423.   
[5] Rauscher T, Applegate J H, Cowan J J, et al. Astrophys J, 1994, 429: 499.   
[6] Balbes M J, Farrell M M, Boyd R N, et al. Nucl Phys, 1995, A584: 315.   
[7] Bai Xixiang, Liu Weiping, Qin Jiuchang, et al. A facility for production and utilization of radioactive beams. Nucl Phys, 1995, A588: 273c.   
[8] Liu W, Li Z, Bai X, et al. Nucl Instrum Methods Phys Res, 2003, B204: 62.   
[9] Zhang Jingshang. Nucl Sci Eng, 2002, 142: 207.

# 中文摘要

利用HI-13串列加速器次级束流线GIRAFFE上产生的能量为 $39\mathrm{MeV}$ 的 ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}$ 次级束，通过逆运动学反应测量了 ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{p})^{9}\mathrm{Lig}_{\mathrm{s}}$ 反应的角分布，导出质心系能量为7.8MeV处反应截面为 $(7.9\pm 2.0)\mathrm{mb}$ 。基于实验数据，利用光学模型，Hauser-Feshbach和激子模型以及DWBA理论计算了反应 ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{p})^{9}\mathrm{Lig}_{\mathrm{s}}$ 的激发函数，进而导出了天体物理反应率随温度的变化关系。

# 5 Astrophysical S-Factor for ${}^{8}\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{p},\gamma)^{9}\mathbf{C}$ via a Study of the ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{p})^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ Reaction

Guo Bing, Li Zhihong, Liu Weiping, Bai Xixiang, Lian Gang, Yan Shengquan, Wang Baoxiang, Zeng Sheng, Su Jun, Lu Yun (China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-46, Beijing 102413, China)

The $^8\mathrm{B}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^9\mathrm{C}$ reaction plays a key role in the $^7\mathrm{Be}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^8\mathrm{B}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^9\mathrm{C}(\alpha ,\mathfrak{p})^{12}\mathrm{N}$ chain which is believed to be a breakout path into the hot CNO cycles[1]. The $^8\mathrm{B}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^9\mathrm{C}$ might be of importance for the evolution of massive stars with very low metallicities where a proton-rich reaction chain is faster than the $3\alpha$ process[1,2]. At the temperatures of astrophysical interest, direct capture is expected to dominate the $^8\mathrm{B}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^9\mathrm{C}$ reaction. This reaction has increasingly attracted theoretical and experimental studies. There were several microscopic and systematic calculations of this reaction that deviated with large uncertainty[1,3,4]. On the experimental side, it is very difficult to directly measure this reaction at energies of astrophysical relevance because of very small cross section and low intensity of the available $^8\mathrm{B}$ beam at present. Some indirect methods have been applied to study this reaction[5-8].

Previously, we measured the $^8\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{d},\mathrm{p})^9\mathrm{Li}_{\mathrm{g.s.}}$ angular distribution with a 39 MeV secondary $^8\mathrm{Li}$ beam in inverse kinematics using the secondary beam facility GIRAFFE[9, 10] at the HI-13 tandem accelerator of China Institute of Atomic Energy. The spectroscopic factor for the ground state of $^9\mathrm{Li} = ^8\mathrm{Li}\otimes \mathfrak{n}$ was extracted to be $0.68\pm 0.14$ . In present work, the asymptotic normalization coefficient (ANC) for the virtual decay $^9\mathrm{Li}\rightarrow{}^8\mathrm{Li} + \mathrm{n}$ was derived to be $(C_{1,3 / 2}^{9\mathrm{Li}})^2 = (1.33\pm 0.27)\mathrm{fm}^{-1}$ .

${}^{9}\mathrm{C}$ and ${}^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ are mirror nuclei, and charge symmetry of nuclear force implies that, to a good approximation, the spectroscopic amplitudes for the proton single particle orbitals in ${}^{9}\mathrm{C}$ wave function are very similar to those of the neutron single particle orbitals in ${}^{9}\mathrm{Li}$ . Consequently, the ANC, $(C_{1,3/2}^{9})^2$ ,

for $^9\mathbf{C} \rightarrow {}^8\mathbf{B} + \mathbf{p}$ can be related to that for $^9\mathrm{Li} \rightarrow {}^8\mathrm{Li} + \mathrm{n}$ ,

$$
\left(C _ {1, 3 / 2} ^ {9 \mathrm {C}}\right) ^ {2} = \left(C _ {1, 3 / 2} ^ {9 \mathrm {L i}}\right) ^ {2} \left(b _ {1, 3 / 2} ^ {9 \mathrm {C}}\right) ^ {2} / \left(b _ {1, 3 / 2} ^ {9 \mathrm {L i}}\right) ^ {2} \tag {1}
$$

The single particle ANCs, $b_{1,3/2}^{9\mathrm{Li}}$ and $b_{1,3/2}^{9\mathrm{C}}$ , can be given by the ratios $u_{l,j}(r)/[W(2k_B r)/r]$ at large radius for $^9\mathrm{Li}$ and $^9\mathrm{C}$ , respectively. It should be noted that the same geometry and the same spin-orbit interaction for Woods-Saxon potentials must be used in the calculations of the single particle wave functions $u_{l,j}(r)$ . We then found $(C_{1,3/2}^{9\mathrm{C}})^2$ to be $(1.10 \pm 0.23) \, \mathrm{fm}^{-1}$ . The error was mainly caused by statistics (18%) and the deviation from optical potentials used in DWBA calculation (9%).

We have also derived the astrophysical $S$ -factor as a function of $E_{\mathrm{c.m.}}$ for the direct capture in the ${}^{8}\mathbf{B}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^{9}\mathbf{C}$ reaction, as shown in Fig.1. The astrophysical factor $S_{18}(0)$ was then found to be $(42\pm 9)$ eV·b. Fig. 2 demonstrates the astrophysical factor $S_{18}(0)$ extracted from theoretical calculations and experiments. Our result is in good agreement with those in Refs.[4-7], but significantly smaller than those reported in Refs. [1,3,8].

![](images/6d8c93a694f75473be213b58fc13ee322c1d47f3d60983bcad6af02c21640474.jpg)  
Fig. 1 Astrophysical $S$ -factor as a function of $E_{\mathrm{c.m.}}$ for the direct capture in ${}^8\mathbf{B}(\mathfrak{p},\gamma)^9\mathbf{C}$

![](images/562e4648894d3d686a755bd93562cb9c17635d4ec3d2f301c476cc616ef56dad.jpg)  
Fig. 2 Astrophysical factor $S_{18}(0)$ extracted from theoretical calculations and experiments

# 中文摘要

利用HI-13串列加速器次级束流线GIRAFFE上产生的 $39\mathrm{MeV}$ 的 ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}$ 次级束，通过逆运动学反应测量了 ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}(d,p)^{9}\mathrm{Li}_{g,s}$ 反应的角分布，通过DWBA分析导出了虚衰变 ${}^{9}\mathrm{Li}\rightarrow{}^{8}\mathrm{Li}+\mathrm{n}$ 的中子ANC， $(C_{1,3/2}^{9\mathrm{Li}})^{2}=(1.33\pm0.27)\mathrm{fm}^{-1}$ 。进而基于镜像核的电荷对称性得出了虚衰变 ${}^{9}\mathrm{C}\rightarrow{}^{8}\mathrm{B}+\mathrm{p}$ 的质子ANC， $(C_{1,3/2}^{9\mathrm{C}})^{2}=(1.10\pm0.23)\mathrm{fm}^{-1}$ 。最后利用辐射俘获理论导出了 ${}^{8}\mathrm{B}(p,\gamma)^{9}\mathrm{C}$ 反应中直接俘获贡献的天体物理S因子随能量的变化关系。

# 6 Production of $^{10}\mathrm{C}$ Secondary Beam

Su Jun, Li Zhihong, Lian Gang, Wang Youbao, Guo Bing, Zeng Sheng, Yan Shengquan, Wang Baoxiang, Bai Xixiang, Liu Weiping (China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-46, Beijing 102413, China)

The inhomogeneous big bang models assume a universe with regions of high-density proton-rich materials surrounded by those of low-density neutron-rich materials, thus many reactions caused by unstable nuclei are very important. Fig.1 shows a part of the nuclear reaction network of primordial nucleosynthesis[1]. The reactions related to $^{10}\mathrm{C}$ are considerable in the investigation of primordial nucleosynthesis. The nuclei close to the driplines are mainly produced through complicated reactions between stable nuclei, while, the driplines can be approached in simple reactions involving radioactive nuclei, for example, the $^{10}\mathrm{C} + \mathrm{p}$ elastic resonance scattering can be used to study the unbound nucleus $^{11}\mathrm{N}^{[2,3]}$ . Furthermore, the measurement of the strength of the superallowed $0^{+} \rightarrow 0^{+}$ transition in the $\beta$ -decay of $^{10}\mathrm{C}$ is important for checking the conserved vector current hypothesis[4,5].

The experiment was carried out using the secondary beam facility[6] of HI-13 tandem accelerator. A 72 MeV $^{10}\mathrm{B}$ primary beam impinged on a hydrogen gas cell at a pressure of 1.6 atm, producing $^{10}\mathrm{C}$

secondary beams through the ${}^{1}\mathrm{H}({}^{10}\mathrm{B},{}^{10}\mathrm{C})\mathrm{n}$ reaction in inverse kinematics. The front and rear windows of the gas cell are Havar foils, each with a thickness of $1.9~\mathrm{mg/cm^2}$ . The ${}^{10}\mathrm{C}$ ions were separated from other series by a dipole magnet. The magnetic rigidity of ion can be expressed as:

$$
B _ {\rho} = \frac {0 . 0 0 3 3 3 5 6}{q} \sqrt {2 M c ^ {2} E _ {k} + E _ {k} ^ {2}} \tag {1}
$$

![](images/c5cc1d43e09f7ff392399b3948a51cd46f247ddb7ec7be0fe652f2231673b69b.jpg)  
Fig. 1 Part of the nuclear reaction network of primordial nucleosynthesis[1]

where $B_{\rho}, q, M$ and $E_{k}$ denote the magnetic rigidity, charge state, mass and kinetic energy of ion, respectively. The strength of the magnetic field was set to match the magnetic rigidity of ${}^{10}\mathsf{C}^{6+}$ . The other ions can be effectively separated since their energies are observably higher than those matching the magnetic rigidity of ${}^{10}\mathsf{C}^{6+}$ , however, their low energy tails can match the magnetic rigidity.

To enhance the purity of the secondary beam, a WIEN filter had been installed on the downstream of the beam line. The force of the ion with the velocity of $\nu$ from the electric $(E)$ and magnetic $(B)$ fields in the WIEN filter is given by

$$
F = q (E - \beta c B) \tag {2}
$$

where $\beta = \nu / c$ , $c$ is the velocity of light. Two apertures with the diameter of $3\mathrm{mm}$ and $5\mathrm{mm}$ had been set up in front of the secondary charge. The strength of the electric and magnetic fields were set to match the velocity of the $^{10}\mathrm{C}^{6+}$ . The deflected distance of the impurity before secondary charge are far bigger than the diameter of the apertures, thus the WIEN filter can effectively enhance the purity of the $^{10}\mathrm{C}$ beam. The secondary beam was detected and identified with a $\Delta \mathrm{E - E}$ counter telescope consisting of a $19.3\mu \mathrm{m}$ silicon $\Delta \mathrm{E}$ and a $300\mu \mathrm{m}$ silicon $\mathrm{E}$ detector.

The two-dimensional spectrum of $\Delta E - E_{\mathrm{t}}$ is shown in Fig.2. The purity of the collimated $^{10}\mathrm{C}$ beam was about $92\%$ after the magnetic and velocity selection, the beam energy was $(55.9\pm 0.9)$ MeV. The intensity of the $^{10}\mathrm{B}$ primary beam can reach to $150~\mathrm{pnA}$ , and the relevant intensity of the $^{10}\mathrm{C}$ secondary beam is $850~\mathrm{s}^{-1}$ , which can meet the requirements of nuclear reaction experiment.

![](images/891e0722a69e64bbeca193f1d8528cdd347426d7382fd0b219269dc7f7c298f5.jpg)  
Fig.2 Scatter plot of $\Delta E$ vs. $E_{\mathrm{t}}$

[3] Markenroth K, Axelsson L, Baxter S, et al. Phys Rev, 2000, C62: 034 308.   
[4] Savard G, Galindo-Uribarri A, Hagberg E, et al. Phys Rev Lett, 1995, 74:1 521-1 524.   
[5] Fujikawa B K, Asztalos SJ, Clark R M, et al. Phys Lett, 1999, B449: 6-11.

# 中文摘要

在中国原子能科学研究院HI-13串列加速器次级束流线上，利用加速器产生的 $72\mathrm{MeV}$ 的 $^{10}\mathrm{B}$ 初级束轰击氢气体靶，通过 ${}^{1}\mathrm{H}({}^{10}\mathrm{B},{}^{10}\mathrm{C})\mathrm{n}$ 逆运动学反应产生了 ${}^{10}\mathrm{C}$ 放射性次级束，经过磁刚度选择以及速度选择，准直后纯度达到 $92\%$ ，能量为 $(55.9\pm 0.9)\mathrm{MeV}$ ，强度约为 $6\mathrm{s}^{-1}\cdot \mathrm{pnA}^{-1}$ 。基本满足在该次级束流线上进行相关核反应实验的要求。

# 7 Study of Neutron Emission Reactions of $^{6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ Induced by Fast Neutrons

Ruan Xichao, Chen Guochang, Zhou Zuying, Li Xia, Huang Hanxiong,

Zhong Qiping, Jiang Jing, Tang Hongqing, Qi Bujia

(China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-46, Beijing 102413, China)

The neutron emission reactions of $^{6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ induced by fast neutrons at 8.17 and 10.27 MeV were investigated by measuring the neutron emission double-differential cross sections. Meanwhile, the measurement results have been analyzed and interpreted with a new light nuclear reaction model.

The experiments were carried out with the normal and abnormal multi-detector fast neutron TOF spectrometer[1,2] on the HI-13 tandem accelerator of CIAE. At 10.25 MeV, the problem on the influence from breakup neutrons of the d-D neutron source on secondary neutron spectra was successfully solved by using the abnormal multi-detector fast neutron TOF spectrometer beside the normal multi

detector fast neutron TOF spectrometer. A deuterium gas target with $10\mathrm{mm}$ in diameter and $30\mathrm{mm}$ in length was used to generate neutrons by D(d, n) reaction. The deuterium gas pressure is about 6 bar. Three liquid scintillator neutron detectors were used to detect the emitted neutron TOF spectrum from sample. The distances from center of gas target to center of sample and from center of sample to center of detector are $18\mathrm{cm}$ and about $600\mathrm{cm}$ , respectively. Twelve angles from 30 to 150 degree of TOF spectra were measured with gas in, sample in and out, gas out, sample in and out runs.

The measured TOF spectra were simulated by the Monte-Carlo code STREUER[3] in a realistic manner. The DDX cross sections were determined by comparing the measured and simulated TOF spectra with selected windows. The cross sections were normalized to n-p scattering cross section.

To describe the reaction behave of light nuclei, especially the particle pre-equilibrium emissions from compound nuclei to the discrete levels of the residual nuclei, the angular momentum dependent exciton model must be used in the reaction model. Therefore, in the light nuclear reaction model the angular momentum coupling effects must be taken into account, as well as the accurate kinematics should also be considered for all kinds of reaction processes. If the secondary particle emissions exist, which are all from discrete levels to discrete levels, or through two body breakup such as $^5\mathrm{He} \rightarrow \mathrm{n} + \alpha$ and $^5\mathrm{Li} \rightarrow \mathrm{p} + \alpha$ , as well as the three-body breakup processes of $^6\mathrm{He} \rightarrow \mathrm{n} + \mathrm{n} + \alpha$ . The pre-equilibrium mechanism dominate the whole reaction processes, even over 80%. Moreover, in light nucleus reactions the recoil effect also has been taken into account exactly to give the correct energy spectra. For $\mathrm{n} + ^6\mathrm{Li}$ reaction, the direct three-body breakup of $\mathrm{n} + \mathrm{d} + \alpha$ has been taken into account, and the systematic formula of direct three-body breakup was established and introduced in this model calculations. The calculated DDX of $\mathrm{n} + ^{6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ were obtained by the new reaction models. All of the fittings agree well with measurements. The analysis and interpretation of the double-differential measurements have been performed[4].

Fig.1 shows our measured elastic data comparing with evaluated data and other measurements. We can see that clear discrepancies exist between different evaluations and measurements, especially at large angles. Fig.2 is the same as Fig.1 but for $^7\mathrm{Li}$ . Fig.3 shows one of the pictures for results of DDX measurement at 10.27 MeV, comparing with the theoretical calculations. Good agreement between measurement and calculation can be seen from the comparison.

![](images/20f392ff0e2cdb5051ac14d152f2f3f1ac8859dbfb4696e488b038bcb14559bc.jpg)  
Fig. 1 Measured elastic differential cross sections comparing with other measurements and different evaluations for $^6\mathrm{Li}$

![](images/475d5e7aa465d037713b6ed91fca9c1f58e480c19365dbb6b75dbbe8e04aa682.jpg)

Fig. 3 DDX result for $^7\mathrm{Li}$ at 10.27 MeV comparing with the calculation

# 中文摘要

通过测量次级中子双微分截面，对8.17MeV与10.27MeV中子与 ${}^{6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ 作用发射中子的反应进行了研究。实验在HI-13串列加速器上的多探测器快中子常规和非常规飞行时间谱仪上进行。对 $10.27\mathrm{MeV}$ ，为消除d-D反应的源破裂中子对次级中子能谱的影响，除采用常规谱仪外，

还采用了非常规谱仪，成功解决了这一难题。为得到正确的注量衰减、多次散射和有限几何修正，对实验数据进行了详细的Monte-Carlo模拟。测量结果用轻核反应理论程序LUNF进行了分析，通过测量与理论计算的比较，得到了满意的结果，理论计算较好地描述了实验数据。

# 8 Double-Differential Neutron Emission Cross Sections Measurements and Theoretical Analysis of $^{6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ at Incident Neutron Energies of 8.17 and 10.27 MeV

Chen Guochang, Ruan Xichao, Zhou Zuying, Zhang Jingshang, Qi Bujia, Li Xia, Huang Hanxiong, Zhong Qiping, Tang Hongqing, Jiang Jing (China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-41, Beijing 102413, China)

# 1 Introduction

The cross section data for neutron induced reactions of $^6\mathrm{Li}$ and $^7\mathrm{Li}$ are the prime requirements of the design of breeding blankets for D-T fueled thermonuclear reactors. Therefore, detailed knowledge of the neutron production cross sections and the secondary neutron spectra of neutron induced reaction of $^6,7\mathrm{Li}$ is required for fusion neutronics calculation[1-4]. Hence the double differential neutron emission cross sections (referred to as DDX here) is very useful, because they include almost all the information that determines the neutron spectra in an assembly. Also, the DDX is one of the most important nuclear data used in nuclear engineering design, particularly in nuclear device and neutron shielding design. In consequence, it can be applied for neutron transport calculations.

The DDX of $^{6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ have been measured at incident neutron energies of 8.17 and 10.27 MeV at CIAE. The differential cross sections and the DDX were calculated and compared with CIAE's measured data.

# 2 Theoretical Interpretations

A suitable approach for light nucleus of the $1P$ shell has been developed and the calculations for $\mathfrak{n} + ^{12}\mathbf{C}^{[5]}$ and $\mathfrak{n} + ^{16}\mathrm{O}^{[6]}$ are successfully performed by analyzing the experimental data. The first-particle emissions can be described through pre-equilibrium and equilibrium mechanisms. The angular momentum coupling effect in pre-equilibrium emission process is proposed for conserving the angular momentum from compound nucleus to the discrete levels of the residual nucleus [6]. The representations of the DDX of sequential particle emissions and the three-body breakup process have been described in Refs.[5-8].

The pre-equilibrium and equilibrium mechanisms were also adopted to describe $\mathfrak{n}^{+6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ reaction processes by taking into account of cross section of every reaction channel, angular distribution and DDX simultaneously. And the LUNF codes for $\mathfrak{n}^{+6}\mathrm{Li}^{[9]}$ and $\mathfrak{n}^{+7}\mathrm{Li}^{[10]}$ reactions are developed for theoretically calculating the reaction cross sections and the energy angular spectra of all kinds of

emission particles in reaction processes below $20\mathrm{MeV}$ . The neutron optical model parameters were obtained by using APOM94 code[11] which is a program for searching automatically an optimum set of neutron optical model parameters.

With considering the finite resolution of the experimental apparatus, we assumed that the expression of the energy resolution function $f(E,E,\sigma)$ is expressed by a Gaussian form as follows:

$$
f (E, \bar {E}, \sigma) = \frac {1}{\sqrt {2 \pi} \sigma} e ^ {- \frac {(E - \bar {E}) ^ {2}}{2 \sigma^ {2}}} \tag {1}
$$

where $\sigma$ is the standard deviation and the representation is as follows:

$$
\sigma = g \frac {\sqrt {W _ {E} ^ {2} + W _ {T} ^ {2} + \Gamma_ {1} ^ {2} + \Gamma_ {2} ^ {2}}}{2 \sqrt {2 \ln 2}} \tag {2}
$$

where $W_{E}$ is the finite energy resolution (in FWHM) of the neutron source, $W_{T}$ the energy spread caused by the finite timing resolution expressed in FWHM, $\Gamma_{1}$ and $\Gamma_{2}$ the secondary particle emission from $k_{1}$ level of compound nucleus to $k_{2}$ level of the residual nucleus, where $\Gamma_{1}$ and $\Gamma_{2}$ represent the level width of $k_{1}$ and $k_{2}$ , respectively. In the above expressions, energy $E$ and $\overline{E}$ is expressed in MeV.

In actually using this formula, the Gaussian width $\sigma$ was multiplied by an adjustable parameter $g$ to account for other broadening sources, such as angular resolution, which contribution to broaden further the spectra. At present experimental condition, here unresolve between inelastic scattering of the first excited state ( $E_{x} = 0.478\mathrm{MeV}$ ) and elastic scattering from ${}^{7}\mathrm{Li}$ , therefore inelastic scattering distribution of the first excited state is overlapped by elastic scattering.

The formulation of the sequential particle emission, the two-body separation, the three-body breakup processes and the DDXs have been described in Refs.[5-8]. The energy balance is strictly considered, which is necessary for the reaction of the light nuclei.

G. G. Ohlsen[12] has described the kinetics of the three-body breakup process. For $\mathfrak{n} + {}^6\mathrm{Li}$ reaction, the direct three-body breakup process of $\mathfrak{n} + {}^6\mathrm{Li}\rightarrow {}^7\mathrm{Li}^*\rightarrow \mathfrak{n}' + \mathfrak{d} + \alpha$ has been taken into account, and the systematic formula of the direct three-body breakup coefficient was established and introduced in presently theoretical calculation. And more details of the systematic formula has described in Ref. [13]. In view of the $\mathfrak{n} + {}^6\mathrm{Li}$ reaction for $E_{\mathrm{n}}\leqslant 20\mathrm{MeV}$ , the reaction channels are as follows:

$$
n + ^ {6} L i = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \gamma + ^ {7} L i & Q = 7. 2 4 9 M e V \\ p + ^ {6} H e & Q = - 2. 7 2 5 M e V \\ d + ^ {5} H e (n + \alpha) & Q = - 1. 4 7 5 M e V \\ t + \alpha & Q = 4. 7 8 2 M e V \\ 2 n + ^ {5} L i (p + \alpha) & Q = - 3. 7 0 0 M e V \\ n, p + ^ {5} H e (n + \alpha) & Q = - 4. 5 9 4 M e V \\ n, d + \alpha & Q = - 1. 4 7 5 M e V \end{array} \right. \tag {3}
$$

The reaction mechanism of $\mathsf{n} + {}^{7}\mathsf{Li}$ is more complex, there are many open reaction channels below 20 MeV. Besides the sequential particle emission, the cluster separation and three-body breakup

processes such as ${}^{6}\mathrm{He}^{*}\rightarrow \mathrm{n} + \mathrm{n} + \alpha$ are also included. In view of the particle emission processes for $\mathrm{n} + {}^{7}\mathrm{Li}$ reaction below $20\mathrm{MeV}$ , all of the particle emit from the compound nucleus of ${}^{8}\mathrm{Li}$ to the discrete levels of the residual nuclei. The representations of DDX of sequential particle emissions and for cluster separation have been obtained in Ref. [5], which are used for the second particle emission processes.

$$
n + ^ {7} L i = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \gamma + ^ {8} L i & Q = 2. 0 3 3 M e V \\ n ^ {\prime} + ^ {7} L i ^ {*} & Q = - 0. 4 7 7 6 M e V \\ d + ^ {6} H e & Q = - 7. 7 5 0 M e V \\ t + ^ {5} H e & Q = - 3. 3 3 6 2 M e V \\ 2 n + ^ {6} L i & Q = - 7. 2 4 9 M e V \\ n, p + ^ {6} H e & Q = - 9. 9 7 4 M e V \\ n, d + ^ {5} H e & Q = - 9. 6 1 8 M e V \\ n, t + a & Q = - 2. 4 6 7 M e V \\ 2 n, p + ^ {5} H e & Q = - 1 1. 8 4 2 M e V \\ 2 n, d + a & Q = - 8. 7 2 4 M e V \end{array} \right. \tag {4}
$$

The theoretical calculation the differential cross sections and the DDXs are compared with the data in evaluation nuclear data libraries, present measurement results and other measurements. First of all, Fig.1 shows the experimental results of differential cross section in comparison with the evaluations and other measurements. Then, Fig.2-6 show the calculation results of DDXs with experimental data, including present measurement results. Also discuss the difference between calculation results and experimental data.

# 3.1 Comparisons of Elastic Angular Distribution

In Fig.1 the elastic angular distributions for $^6\mathrm{Li}$ and $^7\mathrm{Li}$ is shown the present experimental results in comparison with evaluated nuclear data and other measurements[14-20]. For $^6\mathrm{Li}$ , present measure

ment result agree fairly well with other measurements and the data in evaluated nuclear data libraries (CENDL-3.0 $^{[14]}$ and ENDF/B-VI $^{[15]}$ ) within the experimental uncertainties, however the data in JENDL-3.3 library $^{[16]}$ are lower than that of other libraries up to $100^{\circ}$ and almost as a plainness above $100^{\circ}$ . For ${}^{7}\mathrm{Li}$ , present experimental result, other measurements and evaluated data agree fairly well with each other up to $65^{\circ}$ except measurement of M. Adel-Fawzy et al. $^{[20]}$ which is larger than others almost in whole angle range, where the distribution cross sections have contained the first inelastic scattering angular distribution. Meanwhile, the evaluated data of JENDL-3.3 $^{[16]}$ agree well with H. H. Hogue et al. $^{[17]}$ and J. A. Cookson et al. $^{[19]}$ measurements and larger than presently measured result, other measurements and evaluated nuclear data of CENDL-3.0 $^{[14]}$ and ENDF/B-VI $^{[15]}$ between $65^{\circ}$ and $180^{\circ}$ . On the other hand, the measurement of P. W. Lisowski et al. $^{[18]}$ are smaller than others above $80^{\circ}$ . The presently measured results agree fairly well with evaluated data of CENDL-3.0 $^{[14]}$ and ENDF/B-VI $^{[15]}$ within the experimental uncertainties.

![](images/af1ec2bb814d784df70f28f00b39ae4f80758b24b192447d7319f8b6d47990b4.jpg)

# 3.2 Comparisons of DDXs

1) $^6\mathrm{Li}$ case

In Fig.2 the DDX comparisons is shown between calculation results and present measurements at $E_{\mathrm{n}} = 8.17$ MeV for outgoing angles from $30^{\circ}$ to $150^{\circ}$ . On the whole, the calculation results agree well

with present measurements. However, it should be noted that the curve of calculation is not smooth enough between elastic peak and the first inelastic scattering peak and the calculation results is lower than measurements, which may be caused by the large level width. The excitation energy of the first level is $2.186\mathrm{MeV}$ ( $I = 24\mathrm{keV}$ ), and the contributions for the DDX at this energy region mostly come from the expansion of the elastic, the first inelastic scattering and ${}^{6}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\mathfrak{d})\alpha$ reaction, where the DDX broadening would be limited for obtaining good fitting results of elastic and the first inelastic scattering.

In Fig.3 the DDX comparisons is shown for the presently experimental results in comparison with calculation at $E_{\mathrm{n}} = 10.27$ MeV for outgoing angles from $35^{\circ}$ to $120^{\circ}$ . As a whole, the fittings agree well with present measurements except in continuum region for outgoing angle up to $80^{\circ}$ . The statistical fluctuation of present measurement is obvious in the secondary neutron emission energy up to $3.5$ MeV, where the experimental data were measured using the abnormal TOF spectrometer. The statistical uncertainty in abnormal geometry setup measurement is not so good as that in the normal geometry setup. And the experimental data were obtained using the normal fast neutron TOF spectrometer down to $3.5$ MeV of the outgoing neutron energy, where the statistics is better. There is the problem on the curve between elastic and the first inelastic scattering peak just like at $E_{\mathrm{n}} = 8.17$ MeV, although it is not so serious.

# 2) $7$ Li case

In Fig.4 it is illustrated that the calculation results compare with present measurements at $E_{\mathrm{n}} = 8.17 \, \mathrm{MeV}$ for outgoing angles from $30^{\circ}$ to $150^{\circ}$ . The disagreement is obvious in the neutron emission energy region between two peaks: one is elastic including the first inelastic scattering and the other is the second inelastic scattering peak where the calculations are lower than experimental data. As an example, the top part as Fig.5 shows the partial spectra of the neutron emission at $8.17 \, \mathrm{MeV}$ for $30^{\circ}$ . The full line is the calculated energy angular spectrum, meanwhile, "Elastic", $k = 1$ to $k = 3$ and "T" are denoted the distributions come from elastic scattering, the first emitted neutron from the compound nucleus $^{8}\mathrm{Li}^{*}$ to the $k_{\mathrm{th}}$ excited state of $^{7}\mathrm{Li}^{*}$ , in addition, $^{7}\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{n},\mathrm{t})^{5}\mathrm{He}$ reaction processes where $^{7}\mathrm{Li}^{*}$ is at 2nd or 3rd excited state, respectively. The situation of $^{5}\mathrm{He}$ is at ground state, then it will spontaneously breakup as $n + \alpha$ . However the reaction process of $^{7}\mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{n},\mathrm{n}^{\prime})\alpha$ is not open where $^{7}\mathrm{Li}^{*}$ is at the 4th excited state for the reason that the threshold energy is $8.532 \, \mathrm{MeV}$ . We can find its contribution for DDX is very larger between two peaks energy region at $E_{\mathrm{n}} = 10.27 \, \mathrm{MeV}$ , as "T=2" in the bottom part as Fig.5 shown.

Meanwhile, the DDX comparison between the calculation results and present measurements at $E_{\mathrm{n}} = 10.27$ MeV for outgoing angles from $35^{\circ}$ to $120^{\circ}$ is illustrated in Fig.6. In general, all of the fittings agree well with present measurement except at the continuum region and the second inelastic scattering peak. The main reason for the disagreement have been described at $E_{\mathrm{n}} = 10.27$ MeV of $^6$ Li part, where the experimental data were measured using an abnormal fast neutron TOF spectrometer up to $3.5$ MeV. However, the fittings agree well with present measurement between two peaks. The partial spectra of the emission neutrons, as an example, are shown in the bottom part of Fig.5. The full line is the calculated energy angular spectrum, meanwhile, "Elastic", $k = 1$ to $k = 4$ , (n, 2n) and "T=1, 2" are denoted the distributions derive from elastic scattering, the first emitted neutron from the

compound nucleus $^8\mathrm{Li}^*$ to the $k_{\mathrm{th}}$ excited state of $^7\mathrm{Li}^*$ , (n, 2n) reaction channel and $^7\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{t})^5\mathrm{He}$ reaction process where the situation of $^7\mathrm{Li}^*$ is up to 3rd ( $T = 1$ ) and the 4th excited state ( $T = 2$ ), respectively. The main distribution for the DDX between two peaks comes from $^7\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}^\prime \mathfrak{t})\alpha$ , where $^7\mathrm{Li}^*$ is at the 4th excited state. So we can find why the calculation results at this region are so small at $E_{\mathrm{n}} = 8.17 \mathrm{MeV}$ , and the calculation results is reasonable.

# 4 Summary

The statistical reaction model has been employed to describe neutron incident $^{6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ reaction processes up to 20 MeV. Moreover, the pre-equilibrium reaction mechanism dominates the whole reaction processes. The most important point is the angular momentum coupling effect in pre-equilibrium mechanism of the particle emissions from the discrete levels of compound nucleus to the discrete levels of the residual nucleus to be taken into account. And the Fermi energy calculation equation was also reviewed and obtained its values for calculation. Meanwhile, the level-broadening, the finite energy resolution of neutron source, the finite time resolution and other broadening sources in measurements were taken into account for fitting energy-angular spectra of the secondary emission neutrons. The three-body breakup process is a special mechanism in the $\mathsf{n} + ^{6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ reactions that are also taken into account in whole reaction process. In this paper, the direct three-body breakup process as $\mathsf{n} + ^{6}\mathrm{Li}\rightarrow \mathsf{n}^{\prime} + \mathsf{d} + \alpha$ has been taken into account, and its systematic formula was established and introduced in this model calculations for $^6\mathrm{Li}$ . The calculated DDXs of $\mathsf{n} + ^{6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ were obtained by the new reaction models, and the calculation results were generally consistent with the present neutron emissions DDXs measurements at $E_{\mathrm{n}} = 8.17$ and 10.27 MeV.

# 中文摘要

理论上基于统一的Hauser-Feshbach和激子模型理论框架来描述轻核核反应过程，利用LUNF程序得到中子入射 $^{6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ 的反应截面和双微分截面。在整个核反应过程中预平衡反应机制是最主要的贡献来源，其所占比重约在 $80\%$ 以上。另外，在轻核核反应过程中考虑了角动量耦合效应，给出较准确的出射中子能谱。 $\mathbf{n} + ^{6}\mathrm{Li}$ 核反应中，建立直接三体崩裂系数的系统学公式，并将其包括在理论模型程序中。由新的理论模型计算得到 $\mathbf{n} + ^{6,7}\mathrm{Li}$ 双微分截面与实验测量数据符合较好，并对实验测量数据进行了理论分析和解释。

# 9 The Methodology Study for Neutron Resonance Parameter Measurement Within 0.1-1.0 MeV by Using HI-13 Tandem Accelerator Facility

Li Xia $^{1}$ , Zhou Zuying $^{1}$ , Ruan Xichao $^{1}$ , Tang Hongqing $^{1}$ , Jiang Jing $^{1}$ , He Guozhu $^{1}$ , Zhu Liping $^{1}$ , Ma Xiaoyun $^{1}$ , Lin Jun $^{2}$

(1. China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-46, Beijing 102413, China;

2. Physics Department, Nanjing University, Nanjing 210093, China)

The neutron total cross sections at resonance energy region are of great importance not only for the design and development of nuclear reactors but also for the basic study of neutron interaction with nuclei. The neutron resonance parameters are also important and often got from fitting the experiment data by theoretical method such as $R$ -matrix. For neutron source in the most resonance energy region from thermal to several keV, we don't have any facilities such as electron linear accelerator or high current proton accelerator in CIAE. With HI-13 facility, we can get the neutron source lower to $10\mathrm{keV}^{[1]}$ . So we can study the methodology of neutron resonance measurement within some tens of keV to 1 MeV.

Total neutron cross-sections are usually measured by a transmission experiment, which is done by placing a sample in a well-collimated neutron beam and measuring the number of neutrons passing through it. The transmission through a sample is measured by taking the ratio of the background corrected counts measured with and without the sample in the beam. In order to reduce the statistical error, the measurement can be optimized by carefully calculating the thickness of the sample and an optimum split of the experiment time[2]. The SAMMY code can be used to fit the experimental data and obtain the resonance parameters[3].

![](images/83757a88a076fb05b70df99de53e4509aab9ecfd231af4f516981bcc9f79e156.jpg)  
Fig.1 The experiment setting

The experiment setting is shown in Fig.1. The neutron source is generated by a combined target which is shown in Fig. 2. The proton beam passing through the Li target can react with the $\mathrm{T(Ti)}$ target, so that we get a denser source. We use a stilbene scintillator detector as the neutron detector. The threshold for neutron detection is about $100\mathrm{keV}$ which suits the time of flight method for this energy region in our experimental conditions. A lithium glass detector is used as the neutron source monitor for normalization. The data acquisition system is based on CAMAC by using Kmax software.

Two samples have been measured, one is aluminum (cylinder with size of $\phi 35\mathrm{mm}\times 24.3\mathrm{mm}$ ) and the other is beryllium (cuboids with size of $100\mathrm{mm}\times 100\mathrm{mm}\times 20.0\mathrm{mm}$ ). The ROOT is used for raw data analysis[4]. The transmission spectra are shown in Fig. 3 and Fig. 4. The data will soon be fitted by SAMMY to obtain the resonance parameters.

![](images/214e11ce6490d10c5204a9d03d295e1f46007abaebf91c262ea99053c6efefc7.jpg)  
Fig. 2 The structure of the combined target

![](images/2950df8abee1adb0a32f349314f83cad1fc75562c9d3d3dd2b2d2b6ddb9d43f9.jpg)  
Fig. 3 The transmission spectrum of aluminum

![](images/fc50c3f8776991c5925ea52c57f258466e3f15fd16d975f7d59e9c5180053f21.jpg)  
Fig. 4 The transmission spectrum of beryllium

We can divide the neutron resonance region into parts and process each region individually. The work in 2005 shows the region within $0.01 - 1.0\mathrm{MeV}$ can be done by using HI-13 Tandem accelerator facility. More works in this region are scheduled in the following years.

# 中文摘要

反应堆谱中子的能量共跨越 $7\sim 8$ 个数量级，这些中子与燃料核、结构材料核、慢化材料核等发生相互作用，因此这些能量中子的反应截面很重要。共振能量区域的中子全截面不仅对核反应堆设计建造重要，对于中子与核的相互作用的研究也同样重要。低能区的中子源通常使用电子直线加速器，也可以利用强流质子加速器通过散裂反应产生大量中子。但国内目前缺少产生低能中子源的设备。中国原子能科学研究院的平台建设项目中增加了一项150MeV质子直线加速器，其主要应用是提供白光中子源。为了以后开展低能端中子截面测量的工作能够顺利进行，我们先利用串列国家实验室的HI-13设备产生一段共振能区的中子 $(10\mathrm{keV}\sim 1\mathrm{MeV})$ 进行实验方法的研究。

透射法是用于获得中子全截面及共振参数的简单实验方法。将样品放置于一束准直中子前，用中子探测器测量透过样品的中子数，然后再分别测量没有样品时的中子束流及中子束完全被隔离时的本底，所得的透射谱即有样和无样时的谱在扣除本底后的比值。全截面可能是平滑的函数，也可能在特定的中子能量处存在共振峰。透射谱通常使用飞行时间方法进行测量。

2005年的实验采用 ${}^{7}\mathrm{Li}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{n})$ 和 $\mathbf{T}(\mathbf{T_i})$ 组合靶以产生足够强的中子源。实验使用镤晶体探

测器作为主探测器，置于正对束流的探测器屏蔽体中。样品置于主探测器与中子源靶之间的中子束准直出口处。中子源监视探测器使用的是锂玻璃，置于中子源靶的背向约 $150^{\circ}$ 的方向。

2005年的实验测量了铝和铍两个样品，得到了两个样品在 $0.1\sim 1.0\mathrm{MeV}$ 能区的透射谱和全截面，结果证实了可以在串列上使用透射法测量 $0.01\sim 1\mathrm{MeV}$ 能区的核素中子反应全截面及中子共振参数。在该能区的其他核素的全截面及共振参数的测量工作将在此基础上展开。

# 10 Partial Fusion Induced by Weakly Bound Projectiles

Liu Zuhua, Ruan Ming, Zhang Huanqiao, Yang Feng, Lin Chengjian, Wu Yuewei (China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-10, Beijing 102413, China)

From the theoretical point of view two different conflicting scenarios have been foreseen, one[1] predicts a fusion enhancement with respect to reactions involving stable nuclei, the other[2] predicts a fusion cross section suppression due to the reaction flux lost in the breakup channel. Recently, Hagino et al.[3] performed an improved coupled-channels calculation with the aim of reconciling the two conflicting scenarios. They predicted a complete fusion cross section enhancement at energies below the Coulomb barrier and a suppression at energies above the Coulomb barrier. These different theoretical predictions call for precise and reliable measurements as a watershed among various theories. The aim of the present work is to analyze the data for $^6\mathrm{Li}$ and $^9\mathrm{Be}$ pointing out the relation between partial and complete fusion at energies above the Coulomb barrier.

It is well known that fusion near and below the Coulomb barrier is strongly affected by the intrinsic degrees of freedom of the interacting nuclei, whose coupling with the relative motion causes an energy splitting of the single uncoupled fusion barrier. This gives rise to a distribution of barrier heights, that manifests as an enhancement of fusion cross sections at energies near and below the Coulomb barrier. Above the Coulomb barrier this effect becomes less important and, at well above the Coulomb barrier, it can be neglected. Fusion cross section of tightly bound nuclei can be satisfactory described with the semi-classical approach formula

$$
\sigma_ {\text {f u s}} (E) = \pi R _ {\mathrm {B}} ^ {2} \left(1 - \frac {V _ {\mathrm {B}}}{E}\right) \tag {1}
$$

where $V_{\mathrm{B}}(R_{\mathrm{B}})$ is the barrier height (radius). We can rewrite Eq. (1) as

$$
\frac {\sigma_ {\text {f u s}} (E) E}{\pi R _ {\mathrm {B}} ^ {2}} = E - V _ {\mathrm {B}} \tag {2}
$$

The $(\sigma_{\mathrm{fus}}E) / (\pi R_{\mathrm{B}}^{2}) = \sigma_{\mathrm{fus}}^{\mathrm{red}}$ is called "reduced fusion cross section". The $\sigma_{\mathrm{fus}}^{\mathrm{red}}$ is a linear function of $(E - V_{\mathrm{B}})$ .

For weakly bound nuclei, the situation is more complicated. Coupling to channels that act as doorways to breakup enhances the sub-barrier fusion cross sections, whereas breakup itself may result

in capture of only a part of the projectile, thus suppressing complete fusion. These two effects may either partially or totally cancel at energies below the barrier. Above the Coulomb barrier, however, the breakup-capture and/or stripping-like process may manifest itself due to the disappearance of the coupling effects. We will therefore discuss the effect of breakup on fusion by comparing experimental complete fusion cross sections to Eq. (2).

In the case of tightly bound nuclei, the breakup probability is always assumed to be very small. Hence, the effect of breakup on fusion should be weak. This is the case for the well bound system $^{16}\mathrm{O} + ^{208}\mathrm{Pb}$ that in the energy region above the barrier, follows rather well the straight line deduced from Eq.(2). Therefore, the $^{16}\mathrm{O} + ^{208}\mathrm{Pb}$ system can be taken as a good reference for the discussion of breakup effects on fusion. While in the cases of weakly bound projectile induced reactions $^6\mathrm{Li} + ^{208}\mathrm{Pb}$ , $^6\mathrm{Li} + ^{209}\mathrm{Bi}$ and $^9\mathrm{Be} + ^{208}\mathrm{Pb}$ , the complete fusion cross sections lie below the straight line defined by the Eq. (2) at the energies above the Coulomb barrier. Therefore, it is seen that complete fusion of weakly bound projectile with heavy target is suppressed.

In the fusion of weakly bound nuclei, two nuclei undergo a complete fusion(CF) and an incomplete or partial fusion (ICF) processes. The total fusion cross section is sum of CF and ICF cross sections. Fig.1 shows the reduced cross sections of total (open symbols) and complete (solid symbols) fusion of the systems $^6\mathrm{Li} + ^{209}\mathrm{Bi}$ and $^9\mathrm{Be} + ^{208}\mathrm{Pb}$ with Eq. (2). It is shown that the reduced total fusion cross sections are in agreement with Eq. (2), which means the loss in the complete fusion are related with the partial fusion, i.e.,

$$
\sigma_ {\mathrm {C F}} + \sigma_ {\mathrm {I C F}} \approx \pi R _ {\mathrm {B}} ^ {2} \left(1 - \frac {V _ {\mathrm {B}}}{E}\right) \tag {3}
$$

Recently, Diaz-Torres et al.[4] investigated the effect of breakup on total fusion using a new continuum discretized coupled channel (CDCC) method. They found that the breakup enhances the total fusion at energies just around the barrier, whereas it hardly affects the total fusion at energies well above the barrier. Thus their theoretical work supports our analysis.

![](images/5383570b5d2258bc3e2d3685c063a4652b75907027dd974b6e390f6b9a5eebfc.jpg)  
Fig. 1 Reduced fusion cross sections as a function of $(E - V_{\mathrm{B}})$ for the systems ${}^{6}\mathrm{Li} + {}^{209}\mathrm{Bi},{}^{9}\mathrm{Be} + {}^{208}\mathrm{Pb},$ and ${}^{16}\mathrm{O} + {}^{208}\mathrm{Pb}$

# References

[1] Hussein M S, Pato M P, Canto L F, et al. Phys Rev, 1992, C46: 377; 1993, 47: 2 398.   
[2] Dasso C H, Vitturi A. Phys Rev, 1994, C50: R12.   
[3] Hagino K, Vitturi A, Dasso C H, et al. Phys Rev, 2000, C61: 037602.   
[4] Diaz-Torres A, Thompson I J, Beck C. Phys Rev, 2003, C68: 044607.

# 中文摘要

部分熔合是一复杂的过程。分析表明，部分熔合很可能发生在靶核的强吸收边缘区域。相对这一区域的分波，弹核破裂后，其一部分(往往是较轻的一块)被靶核俘获，而另一部分飞离靶核的势场成为出射粒子。我们通过 ${}^{6}\mathrm{Li} + {}^{208}\mathrm{Pb}$ ， ${}^{9}\mathrm{Be} + {}^{208}\mathrm{Pb}$ ， ${}^{6}\mathrm{Li} + {}^{209}\mathrm{Bi}$ ， ${}^{9}\mathrm{Be} + {}^{209}\mathrm{Bi}$ 4个反应系统的实验激发函数与经典理论预言的比较，直观地显示了在垒上能区，弹核破裂使全熔合截面减少，而弹核破裂对总熔合截面几乎没有影响。此外，我们还得出了全熔合截面与部分截面之间的关系。

# 11 Study of Barrier Distributions for $^{32}\mathrm{S} + ^{90,96}\mathrm{Zr}$

Yang Feng, Lin Chengjian, Wu Xiukun, Zhang Chunlei, Zhang Huanqiao, Liu Zuhua (China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-10, Beijing 102413, China)

The coupling between the relative motion and the internal degrees of freedom of the colliding nuclei gives rise to a distribution of fusion barriers. Information on the nature and strengths of the couplings lies in the distribution of fusion barriers. The experimental determination of this distribution will provide a stringent test for subbarrier fusion models. We will extract the barrier distributions from the excitation functions measured for quasielastic scattering of $^{32}\mathrm{S} + ^{90,96}\mathrm{Zr}$ around the Coulomb barrier at backward angle with high precision in small energy steps and compare with Coupled channel calculations using the CCFULL code to study the effects of phonon excitation and neutron transfer couplings.

Two experiments were carried out with collimated $^{32}\mathrm{S}$ beam from the HI-13 tandem accelerator at CIAE, Beijing. The measurements were carried out over the bombarding energy range of 80-122 MeV in the step of about 1 MeV. Two Si(Au) detectors and five $\Delta \mathrm{E - E}$ detector telescopes positioned at backward angles from 150 to 170 degree. Quasi-elastic cross sections have been measured for $^{32}\mathrm{S} + ^{90,96}\mathrm{Zr}$ with high accuracy and the barrier distributions were extracted from them(Fig. 1). We find that barrier distribution of $^{32}\mathrm{S} + ^{96}\mathrm{Zr}$ is flat and extends to low energies. The sub-barrier fusion cross sections are strongly enhanced because of such barrier distribution. Compared with the reaction of $^{32}\mathrm{S} + ^{90}\mathrm{Zr}$ , $^{32}\mathrm{S} + ^{96}\mathrm{Zr}$ has stronger neutron transfers up to the six-neutron pickup, which is due to the positive $Q$ values of neutron transfers in the latter. This indicates that the neutron transfer may play a role in the enhancement of sub-barrier fusion for $^{32}\mathrm{S} + ^{96}\mathrm{Zr}$ .

![](images/50d744d5561984cbb0c72e20766b499388931d05b01fe31b9eb20351c5fd896e.jpg)

Fig. 1 Representations of the barrier distribution for $^{32}\mathrm{S} + ^{90,96}\mathrm{Zr}$ systems (solid circles) compared with the CCDEF calculations   
![](images/9f8de15ed4ea86d37b339a82a0e1deb5a8238e8173cf91e6993e33e13adff1ee.jpg)  
The different curves correspond to CCFUL calculations: UNCOUPLED represents the uncoupled case, COUPLED1, COUPLED2 and COUPLED3 for $^{32}\mathrm{S} + ^{90}\mathrm{Zr}$ includes coupling to several inslatic excitations of $^{32}\mathrm{S}$ and $^{90,96}\mathrm{Zr}$ , respectively, and the solid line cases coupling to all neutron transfer channels of the positive $Q$ values were added

# 中文摘要

在背角大于 $150^{\circ}$ 以后的7个角度，精确测量了 ${}^{32}\mathrm{S} + {}^{90,96}\mathrm{Zr}$ 的背角准弹散射激发函数，束流能量范围为 $80\sim 122\mathrm{MeV}$ ，能量步长为 $1\mathrm{MeV}$ 左右，统计误差小于 $1\%$ 。同时，我们通过ESIC程序计算了这个能区的弹性散射激发函数，和实验结果符合较好。从实验结果中分别抽取了它们的势垒分布，对比观测到 ${}^{32}\mathrm{S} + {}^{96}\mathrm{Zr}$ 的势垒结构扁平且向低能区展宽，这种势垒可导致在垒下能区该体系的熔合截面的大。经过与CCDEF计算结果的比较，可以看出声子激发道和多中子转移道分别对熔合反应截面有着很大的影响。

# 12 An Experimental Study on Long-Range Angular Correlation in Excitation Functions for the Reaction System of $^{19}\mathbf{F} + {}^{27}\mathbf{Al}$

Wang Qi $^{1}$ , Han Jianlong $^{1}$ , Dong Yuchuan $^{1}$ , Li Songlin $^{1}$ , Duan Limin $^{1}$ , Wu Heyu $^{1}$ , Xu Huagen $^{1}$ , Chen Ruofu $^{1}$ , Xu Hushan $^{1}$ , Bai Zhen $^{1}$ , Li Zhichang $^{2}$ , Lu Xiuqin $^{2}$ , Zhao Kui $^{2}$ , Liu Jiangcheng $^{2}$ , Zhou Ping $^{2}$

(1. Institute of Modern Physics, Chinese Academy of Sciences, Lanzhou 730000, China;

2. China Institute of Atomic Energy, P. O. Box 275-10, Beijing 102413, China)

An experiment on excitation functions for the dissipative collisions of $^{19}\mathrm{F} + {}^{27}\mathrm{Al}$ has been performed at the HI-13 tandem accelerator of the China Institute of Atomic Energy, Beijing. The incident beam $^{19}\mathrm{F}$ energies were from 110.25 to $118.75\mathrm{MeV}$ in steps of $250\mathrm{keV}$ . A self-supporting $^{27}\mathrm{Al}$ foil with a thickness of about $60~\mu \mathrm{g / cm}^2$ was used as a target. In the experiment 6 sets of $\Delta \mathrm{E - E}$ telescope were set at $57^{\circ}$ , $30^{\circ}$ , $15^{\circ}$ , $-15^{\circ}$ , $-30^{\circ}$ and $-53^{\circ}$ , respectively, in laboratory system to identify the projectile-like fragments. Two silicon detectors were placed at $\pm 7.5^{\circ}$ to monitor the beam direction.

We are devoted to study the long-range angular correlation in excitation functions for the dis

sipative products induced from $^{19}\mathrm{F} + {}^{27}\mathrm{Al}$ , especially to test a theory prediction[1] on an existence of a maximum value of the correlation between two angles separated $180^{\circ}$ in the center of mass system.

The experimental datum analysis is in progress. A velocity plot is presented in Fig.1 for the experimental detection system and the projectile-like fragments induced from the dissipative reaction of $^{19}\mathrm{F} + {}^{27}\mathrm{Al}$ at $114\mathrm{MeV}$ . As shown in the velocity plot the two angles of $57^{\circ}$ and $-30^{\circ}$ in laboratory system are symmetry for the projectile-like fragments O and N in the center of mass system.
